# GTC-449 — Contact import, the screen: a "decide later" state for a child, then the contacts page, picking and the prompt

> Filed by Claude Code on 2 Nov 2026, from the founder's brief and his rulings
> on the plan (2 Nov 2026). Planned and ruled for Stages 1 and 2; Stage 3 is an
> outline only. Not built. Branch `feat/contact-import-screen`, cut locally
> from origin/main `128b341` (no upstream, no commit, no push). The ticket is
> staged and not committed.

**Status:** Open. One ticket, three stages, ruled as one (ruling 11). It stays
open until Stage 3 lands, which the founder expects to take weeks. Stages 1
and 2 are ONE branch and ONE pull request and deploy together (ruling 18);
Stage 3 gets its own later branch and pull request. Stage 1 (the marker and
its migration) is built first; the founder approved its SQL (ruling 57), and
the schema and migration were committed as `298c9c1` once the S1.3 rehearsal
came back green (rulings 58 and 59). Stage 3 cannot be planned until A5 is
ruled and the points under "What the Stage 3 plan must settle" are settled;
its design points are ruled (rulings 36 to 45, amended by rulings 51 and 52)
and its outline folds in the founder's run sheet. Nothing is built.

### Where this comes from — the brief

The founder's first message, the WHY, verbatim:

> I am adding a way to bring contacts into Gather (run sheet steps in a
> roadmap; the reading code is built and merged as GTC-448). When a host
> picks a loaded contact into a Kid with a job row, and that person
> has a number or email, Gather must tell her and offer three options:
> message, do not message, or decide later. Decided today: adults get
> name, email and phone filled in; children get name only; picking never
> ticks Old enough to message directly. And I chose to build all three
> options. Today the first two exist (the tick on means message, saved
> as an adult role but still shown as a kid with a job; the tick off
> means do not message, saved as a child, with any number still stored
> unless the screen blanks it first). Decide later does not exist, and
> an earlier code check said adding it needs a database change.

The safe default he asked for, verbatim: *"a decide later child is never
messaged, and nothing already-working changes for adults or for children who
are already saved."*

The safety lines of that message, verbatim, which become this ticket's Stops:

> NOTHING SENDS FOR REAL. Every test stubs the transports. GATHER_LIVE_
> SENDS only through liveBehindTrap. Never call TNZ, and never run
> scripts/test-tnz-sms.ts. Never connect to production. Never print,
> echo, log or paste any key or secret from the .env files, or the
> database connection string. Tests never delete rows they didn't
> create.
>
> Zones, as GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md names them (Do-Not-Touch Zones):
> Zones 7 and 9 are read-only; nothing under src/app/api/auth/ changes;
> Zone 6 (the security suite, tests/security-*.ts, and scripts/triage-
> unknown-routes.ts) is not touched. No migration and no new API route
> without Nigel's ruling (Zone 5's steps for a migration; his approval
> for a new route).

### The shape of this ticket: three stages

Ruled as one ticket (ruling 11). Stages 1 and 2 are built on this branch, in
order, and go to main in ONE pull request and deploy in ONE deploy (ruling 18,
which supersedes the last clause of ruling 8, "the migration ships alone as its
own deploy first", and ruling 11's "shipped alone first"). Stage 3 gets its own
later branch and pull request.

- **Stage 1 — the marker and its migration.** One added yes/no column on the
  person-in-an-event row, default no. A tripwire test that no sending, link or
  counting code reads it. Built first and held at Zone 5 until the founder
  approves the SQL. It no longer deploys on its own. Also in scope, by ruling
  64: one amendment to `tests/sample-marker-test.ts`, its check M1, which asked
  the GTC-426 migration to be the last one; this ticket's migration now follows
  it. Also in scope, by ruling 72: one data amendment to
  `tests/tweaks-guard-test.ts`, its list of ruled changes (checks G17 and G18),
  which holds Zone 5 at `21a9577` plus GTC-426's change and did not list this
  ticket's.
- **Stage 2 — the household form, the settle blanking, the confirm line, the
  pre-flight lines, the card marker and the tests.** The "Decide later" choice
  beside the tick; the three save doors write the marker; the form blanks the
  held details when a decide-later child is settled to "don't message", and
  the server keeps the email if the person has an unsubscribe on any event
  (rulings 33 and 34); a
  confirm line before a decide-later child is saved to "message" after the
  invitations have gone; a read-only line in pre-flight step 3 and its
  closed-row line; the marker on the household card; the tests. (The
  `test:child-assignment` script is the first commit of the branch, S0, ruling
  24, before Stage 1.)
- **Stage 3 — the contacts page, loading, picking and the prompt.** Outline
  only. Not planned until A5 is ruled and the plan has settled the points
  listed under "What the Stage 3 plan must settle". Its own later branch and
  pull request, merged with a merge commit (ruling 49).

### What the code does today (Claude Code, read 2026-11-02 at `128b341`)

Code is cited by symbol, not by line number. The plan's line numbers are left
out on purpose (house style; they rot).

**Where the child-or-adult status lives.**

- `PersonEvent.householdRole` in `prisma/schema.prisma`, type `HouseholdRole`:
  PRIMARY_CONTACT, PARTNER, GUEST, CHILD, or empty (empty means an adult added
  directly, not through a household). It is the only messaging gate:
  `src/lib/eligibility/child-exclusion.ts` (Moment 4 §10.6, "absolute").
- `PersonEvent.isYoungPerson`, yes/no, default no. Display only ("captured as a
  kid with a job"). Every kid row gets yes, whatever its role.
- "Old enough to message directly" is **not stored**. It is a tick in the form
  (`Moment1InputForm.tsx`, the helper rows), sent as `adultRoled`. The save
  turns it into a role: ticked is GUEST, not ticked is CHILD
  (`createMember` in `households/route.ts`; `reconcileHouseholdMembers` in
  `src/lib/households/reconcileMembers.ts`). On reload the tick is rebuilt as
  "the role is not CHILD" (`apiHouseholdToSaved` in `setup/page.tsx`).
- Three save doors take it: a new household (`POST households`), an edited
  household (`PUT households/[householdId]`, through
  `reconcileHouseholdMembers`) and her own household
  (`host-household/route.ts`, through `hostHousehold.ts`). Each payload type
  carries `adultRoled`.
- A child's number and email sit on `Person` (`phoneNumber`, `email`) whatever
  the role. A save never wipes them. A kid with the tick off is stored with
  whatever number was typed, as the brief says.
- The household routes return members with Prisma `include`, so a new column on
  `PersonEvent` reaches their responses without edits. The pre-flight route
  names each field it returns, so the marker would have to be added there.
- An unsaved household is kept in the browser for seven days
  (`src/lib/households/form-draft.ts`, GTC-435), kids' numbers included. Its
  row check (`isRow`) and restore would have to carry a new field.

**The two shapes of the child gate.** This is why the role stays CHILD.

- Allowlist shape — "is one of the three adult roles or empty":
  `isMessageableRole`, `MESSAGEABLE_PERSON_EVENT`, `NON_CHILD_PERSON_EVENT`. A
  new role value would be blocked by these automatically.
- Equality shape — "is exactly CHILD": `isChildMembership`, and direct tests of
  `householdRole === 'CHILD'`. A new role value would be treated as an adult by
  these: `ensureEventTokens` in `tokens.ts` (it would be minted a guest link),
  `isClaimableMembership` in `shared-link-exposure.ts` (public family directory
  and claim), the refusal in `resend.ts`, the board (`glance/read.ts`,
  `glance/actions.ts`, `glance/replay-entry.ts`), `saveContactBeforePress` in
  `contact-fix.ts`, the headcount in `src/lib/ai/plan-input.ts` (counted as an
  adult in food quantities), `kidsNote`, `isKidWithJob` in `moment3/people.ts`,
  `HOUSEHOLD_ROLE_ORDER` in `glance/state.ts`, the pre-flight route's role
  order, and four places in `setup/page.tsx`.
- The security suite (Zone 6) asserts that children are not exposed. A fifth
  role value would need it extended, and Zone 6 is closed.

**Every place that decides who gets messaged.** Under the ruled design the
marker is read by none of them; a decide-later child is a CHILD in all of them.

- The Send press: `pressSend` in `press.ts`, `readAskPreview` and
  `askRowPopulation`. A child is not asked directly; their item ask rides in
  the message to the household's contact (the "carrier"). If that route is
  closed, the child goes to the host's list. No message to the child.
- The outbound cron (every 2 minutes): `drainOnce` in `dispatch.ts` asks the
  chooser again at send time (the gates run first).
- Reminders: the nudges cron (every 15 minutes) queues rows only
  (`runNudgeScheduler`, `findNudgeCandidates` in `nudge-eligibility.ts`,
  through `chooseChaseRoute`). A child's chase goes to the carrier.
- Wrap-up thank-you: `selectWrapUpRecipients` in `wrap-up.ts`; sent by the
  wrap-up-dispatch cron. Children are filtered out.
- Decide-by follow-up: `findDecideByFollowupCandidates` in
  `decide-by-eligibility.ts`; its role check is authoritative and skips with a
  recorded reason. Cron every 15 minutes.
- By-hand nudge: `resolveManualNudgeRecipient` in `manual-nudge-recipient.ts`
  refuses with 403. The board hides the door.
- Resend of a failed message: refused, CHILD_NOT_MESSAGED.
- Guest links: a child's link is never minted and an old one is revoked
  (`ensureEventTokens`); the public family directory and the stranger-claim
  route exclude children (`SHAREABLE_PERSON_EVENT`, `isClaimableMembership`).
- STOP and unsubscribe (Zones 7 and 9): a STOP is kept by phone number across
  the whole account (`opt-out-service.ts`); an email unsubscribe is by link
  token. Neither looks at the role. A child who is never messaged never sends
  STOP. Nothing in these changes and no zone file is touched.
- `tnz-status-poll` reads delivery reports only; it picks no one.
- Sign-in and welcome emails go to signed-in hosts, never to household members.

**The one new consequence.** `enrolMiniSends` in `dispatch.ts`, run by the
2-minute cron, queues an ask for any messageable person on an already-pressed
event who has none. So flipping a decide-later child to GUEST after the
invitations have gone makes Gather message them shortly after the save, for
real where live sending is on. Today's tick already does this; decide later
makes it the common path. That is why ruling 5 asks for a confirm line.

**Pre-flight.** Five steps; step 3 is "Who Gather talks to"
(`PREFLIGHT_STEP_TITLES`). Step 3 already prints "Kids: Josh, Mere" per
household (`kidsNote`) and greys child rows (`mark-rows.tsx`). Send is greyed
until the steps are ticked. Each tick is stamped with a fingerprint of what
the step showed (`preflightPrints` in `tick-memory.ts`), and the tick goes if
that changes elsewhere. After the press the page is replaced by the smaller
`AfterThePress` screen. Nothing today tells "she chose not to message" from
"she never thought about it": the tick off is also the default.

**Production.** Railway auto-deploy has been off since 2026-08-10; deploys are
by hand. `npm run build` is: the live-sends check, `prisma generate`, `prisma
migrate deploy`, `next build`. So a migration reaches the production database
during the build, before the new code serves, and the old code keeps running
meanwhile. Precedents for this ticket's shape: `isYoungPerson` was added as an
additive yes/no column with a default (migration
`20260804082756_gtc172_household_contact_channel`); `CHILD` itself was added
to the role list in `20260414050000_add_child_role_rename_littlecount`.

**Ticket numbers (checked on all 11 origin branches, local branches and this
folder, 2026-11-02).** Taken from 448 up: 448 only (main). 441 to 447 exist
only on `origin/feat/demo-public`. 440 is the founder's sign-in request,
untracked in his other working folder. 449 and up: free on every ref visible.
GTC-448's ruling 22 says "No GTC-449 for now"; it reads as "no ticket for the
sandbox work", not a reservation. Ruling 9 takes GTC-449.

### Contact details Gather will not use — said plainly

Ruling 2 holds a child's number on the child's row while the choice is decide
later, and ruling 15 says that "number" there means contact details: the number
AND the email, under one rule. The rule: **held on the row for decide later,
left out for don't message, never used until the host chooses message.**

**While a child is on decide later, the row holds contact details that Gather
will not use.** The child is stored as CHILD; every gate above treats the row
as a child; nothing that sends, links or counts will read the new marker. The
marker never lets the details be used. It records only that the host has not
settled the question. They are used only if the host chooses message: the row
then becomes an adult (GUEST) by the existing path, and the tick is hers.

What this costs, stated so it is a choice and not an accident: a young person's
number and email are kept that no Gather path will contact. Who can see them:
the host and co-host on the household and pre-flight screens; they are never
published (the directory and links exclude children).

How long they are kept (ruling 17): when the host settles a decide-later child
to "don't message" in the household form, the form blanks the held number and
email, so only a decide-later child holds contact details. One exception
(rulings 33 and 34): if the person has an `EmailOptOut` row on any event, the
server refuses to clear the email and keeps it, and the phone is still
cleared. Which save door and which part of the form does the blanking, and how
the server recognises the settle, is in step S2.3.

What ruling 17's sentence does not reach, said so it is not read as more than
it is: it is true of the flows this ticket builds. It is not true of children
saved before this ticket, nor of a kid row typed with the tick off and Decide
later off, which keep whatever they hold, as today (ruling 19). Stage 2 blanks
nothing except the ruling-17 settle. The founder confirmed this reading
(ruling 31): ruling 17 covers the flows this ticket builds. No backfill.

**In plain words, the blanking can reach another host's guest list (ruling
27).** Gather does not keep one copy of a person per event. When a host types
a row, the person is matched by email address and first name, "on this event
or another" (GTC-293, the ruled trade), and the match is not limited to one
host. So one person record can sit behind rows in several events, including
events run by other hosts. Blanking a decide-later child's email and phone
writes to that shared record. Anyone else's event row that uses the same
person then shows no email and no number for them. If that is another host's
adult guest, that host's guest list has changed, without that host doing
anything. The founder accepted this (ruling 27): it is what the edit door
already does for every edit of a person's details. A test pins the behaviour
(see the tests). One consequence of that sharing is named as its own risk
below (Named risk 1, ruling 30).

### Named risk 1 — an unsubscribed address can be emailed again (30, 33, 34)

Found by reading only, 2026-11-02, at `128b341`. Nothing was run, and no Zone 9
file was touched. The risk is named here. **Ruled: option A1 (ruling 33),
widened to an opt-out on any event (ruling 34)**, which closes it for the
settle path; see "The ruling" at the foot for exactly what that does and does
not cover. The choices as put to the founder are kept below, so the ruling can
be read against them.

**The finding, in plain words.** Yes, it can happen, but only if all four of
these hold:

1. The guest unsubscribed by clicking the link in the email. (A guest who
   reported the email as spam instead is also blocked by address, everywhere,
   and is not exposed: see "What is already safe".)
2. That guest's email address is then removed or changed on their stored
   person record. Any edit through the household edit door can do this.
3. The same address is then typed on a NEW row in the same event where they
   unsubscribed: a new member, an import, a people add. Gather finds no
   person holding that address, makes a new person, and the new person carries
   no unsubscribe. (Typed into the row it came from, the old person gets the
   address back and the unsubscribe still applies.)
4. That event is still sending email to the new row. Before the press it joins
   the press; after the press the late-arrival sweep queues it.

Then Gather emails the address that unsubscribed from that event. The guest can
unsubscribe again from the new email's link.

**The same risk exists today.** Yes. The household edit door already writes a
blank email when a host clears an email box, for any member, a child included.
So the sequence needs no settle and no shared person: one host clears a guest's
email, then types it again on a new row. What this ticket changes is how likely
the steps are. The settle (ruling 17) makes blanking an intended act for every
settle that holds an email. Ruling 27 lets one host's settle land on a person
that another host's event uses, which starts step 2 from outside the event.
Stage 3's picking will make step 3 (a new row made from a pick) routine.

**How an email unsubscribe is stored and checked.**

- Stored: one `EmailOptOut` row keyed by the person and the event
  (`@@unique([personId, eventId])`), with the time and the token that was
  presented. It holds **no address.** It is written by the `POST` handler in
  `src/app/api/unsubscribe/[token]/route.ts` (an upsert on the person and
  event named in the signed token; `readUnsubscribeToken` in
  `unsubscribe-token.ts`, whose token carries a person id and an event id and
  nothing else), and, for a spam report, by the `COMPLAINED` branch of
  `pollOne` in `src/lib/email-delivery/delivery-poll.ts`.
- Checked, by person and event and never by address. `readAskPreview` in
  `ask-preview.ts` loads the event's opted-out person ids once with
  `listEmailOptOutsForEvent` and marks each member with `emailOptedOutFact`;
  `askChannelOf` and `chaseChannelOf` in `channel-chooser.ts` refuse on that
  fact before they look at the address. The press (`pressSend`), the outbound
  drain (`drainOnce`) and the late-arrival sweep (`enrolMiniSends`) all take
  their answer from `readAskPreview`. The other senders ask
  `getEmailOptOut(personId, eventId)`: `dispatchPendingWrapUpMessages` and
  `retryUndeliveredThankYous` (`wrap-up.ts`), `retryUndeliveredFollowups`
  (`decide-by-sender.ts`), `findDecideByFollowupCandidates`
  (`decide-by-eligibility.ts`), `resolveSubject` in `resend.ts`, and the
  by-hand nudge route.
- The one check by address is the provider's block, `EmailBlock`, read through
  `listEmailBlocks` in `email-block.ts` and applied to the address on the
  person's record as it is now.
- So: an unsubscribe is by person and event; a block is by address; a spam
  report writes both (`recordEmailBlock` plus the opt-out); a hard bounce or a
  suppression writes the block only.

**How a person is picked or made when a household is saved.**
`findOrCreateCapturedPerson` in `src/lib/households/capturePerson.ts`, called
for every new row (`createMember` in `households/route.ts`, `createNewMember`
in `reconcileHouseholdMembers`, and the people and batch-import `POST`s):

- With an email: take every person whose email equals it, ignoring capitals (on
  a sample event, only people already on that sample); keep those whose first
  name matches the typed one (the first word, lower-cased); of those take the
  host's own person, else one already on this event, else the lowest id. The
  match is not limited to one host or one event ("on this event or another",
  GTC-293).
- If none is kept, or the row has no email, make a new person. A row without an
  email always makes a new person; phone numbers are not matched.
- An existing row is not matched again. `updateExistingMember` updates that
  row's person in place, by `personEventId`, writing `email: member.email ||
  null` and the phone, so a blank box becomes null on the person.
- Removing a row (the removal loop in `reconcileHouseholdMembers`) deletes the
  membership and not the person, so the person and their unsubscribe stay.

**The sequence, step by step.**

1. The click: the `POST` handler in the unsubscribe route writes
   `EmailOptOut(P, E)` for person P and event E.
2. The blank: `updateExistingMember` sets P's email to null, through the edit
   door (today by a host clearing the box; with this ticket also the settle).
3. The retype on a new row: `createNewMember` or `createMember` calls
   `findOrCreateCapturedPerson`; no person holds the address, so
   `db.person.create` makes P2.
4. The send: `readAskPreview` builds E's opted-out set, P2 is not in it, and no
   `EmailBlock` holds the address; `askChannelOf` returns email; `pressSend`,
   `enrolMiniSends` and `drainOnce` send to the address.

**What is already safe.**

- Texts. The text opt-out is looked up on the phone number across the whole
  account (`SmsOptOut`, read by `readAskPreview` from the roster's numbers), so
  a number blanked and typed again finds its STOP.
- A spam report: it also writes an `EmailBlock` on the address, so the address
  stays blocked whichever person carries it. Bounces and suppressions block the
  address too.
- Another event: an unsubscribe is a no to one event only (GTC-296 ruling 1),
  so a new event was never covered. Nothing is lost there.
- Typing the address into the row it came from.

**What would close it** (the founder's choice; none is decided here; least
change first):

- **A. Refuse to blank where it would leave an unsubscribe behind.** In
  `updateExistingMember` (`reconcileMembers.ts`), skip nulling the email when
  the person has any `EmailOptOut` row. Variants: A1, only on the settle path,
  leaving today's manual clear as it is; A2, on every blank, which closes
  today's path too but changes ordinary edits (a host could no longer clear an
  unsubscribed guest's email); A3, skip on any other membership at all, which
  also closes ruling 27's reach but would rarely blank a returning household,
  because the same person sits in last year's event, so ruling 17 would mostly
  not apply. The phone needs no guard, since its opt-out is on the number.
  Cost: one read and one rule in one file; the form shows blank but the server
  keeps the email, so a reloaded form shows it again, which needs a sentence
  worded (a ruling); item 12 flips. Zones: none edited. A1 and A2 read Zone
  9's table from non-zone code; A3 reads only memberships.
- **B. Check the opt-out by address as well as by person.** The opt-out row
  holds no address and the blanking removes the only copy, so this needs an
  address stored on `EmailOptOut`: a schema change to a Zone 9 model and a
  Zone 5 migration, changes to both writers (the unsubscribe route, `pollOne`)
  and to every reader (`email-opt-out.ts`, `readAskPreview`, the chooser's
  context, wrap-up, decide-by, `resend.ts`, the by-hand nudge), and a backfill
  that cannot recover addresses already blanked. Zones: 9 (read-only) and 5.
  The largest, and not possible without lifting Zone 9.
- **C. Do not blank the shared record: give the child's row its own person
  without details.** It leaves the shared person, its address and its
  unsubscribe untouched, and closes ruling 27's reach as well. But a person
  carries the child's items (assignments), links (tokens) and nudge history,
  which would have to move to the new person, and it reopens GTC-293's identity
  rule. Zones: the Zone 3 neighbourhood (`AccessToken` hangs on the person).
  Large.
- **D. Accept it, as today.** Nothing to build. The risk stays named and open;
  item 12 pins the outcome, so it is a known behaviour and not a surprise.
  Zones: none.

Least change that closes it, as put: A1, then A2. B and C cost far more.

**The ruling (rulings 33 and 34): A1, on any event.** When she settles a
decide-later child to "don't message", the server refuses to clear the email if
that person has an `EmailOptOut` row on ANY event; the email is kept. Ruling 33
said "for this event"; ruling 34 corrected it to any event, the shared-person
case being the reason. How the server tells a settle, what it does, and how the
any-event read is done is in S2.3. Today's manual clear of an email on any
ordinary row stays exactly as it is.

- **Closed:** the settle path, whichever event holds the person's unsubscribe,
  this one or another host's. Item 12 of "The tests" checks it. What it costs:
  a person who unsubscribed from any event, even an old one, keeps their email
  at a settle; that is rare, and it is the safe direction.
- **Open, a later item to close with A2 in a later ticket:** today's ordinary
  clear. A host who clears an unsubscribed guest's email on a plain row, or on
  a decide-later row that stays decide-later, still nulls it, as before. After
  a refused settle the kid is a plain row, so a second save that clears its
  email is that ordinary clear and is not refused.
- **Settled, was flag 1 (ruling 34):** ruling 33 said "for this event", which
  would have missed the shared-person case: host A's settle, in A's event,
  finds no row for A's event and clears the email, and host B's guest
  (unsubscribed on B's event) could then be retyped into a new person and
  emailed. Ruling 34 widens it to any event. The any-event read needs no Zone
  9 edit: Zone 9's readers are per event (`getEmailOptOut` takes a person and
  an event; `listEmailOptOutsForEvent` takes an event), so the question "does
  this person have an opt-out on any event" is asked by one direct query from
  `updateExistingMember`, in non-zone code. Precedent: `glance/rewind.ts` and
  the unsubscribe page query the table directly too. The query is in S2.3.
  Test item 12(c) now pins that an opt-out only on another event keeps the
  email.

### Not in this ticket

- Any edit to a sending gate, the chooser, token issuance, the directory, the
  claim route, the opt-out files (Zones 7 and 9), the auth routes (Zones 1 and
  2), or the security suite (Zone 6).
- A fifth role value, a date marker or a separate table (ruling 1 rules them
  out).
- A one-tap settle, and any new API route (ruling 4).
- A block on sending (ruling 3).
- Merging duplicate contacts (GTC-448 left it undecided).
- Supporting overseas numbers (they are flagged, never supported).
- **STOP handling** (ruling 50, B8). No change. Nothing in Stage 2 or Stage 3
  touches it: not `SmsOptOut`, not `Person.smsOptedOut`, not
  `opt-out-service.ts`, not the TNZ webhook.
- The `AfterThePress` screen. A decide-later child is not listed there; the
  household form is the place after the press. That is a stated limit.
- Stage 3 in detail.
- Any other change to `tests/sample-marker-test.ts`, and any other over-tight
  pin that turns up at a gate: each comes back to the founder one by one and is
  never fixed in a batch (ruling 64).
- Any other change to `tests/tweaks-guard-test.ts`: the guard keeps holding
  every other protected path and span byte for byte (ruling 72).

### Stops

**Read with rulings 95 and 97 (3 Nov 2026).** Ruling 71's stop on a connection
to any other database is read together with rulings 95 and 97, on the
throwaway copy.

- **Zone 5, Stage 1.** Generate the migration with `--create-only
  --skip-generate`, surface the SQL, and hold BOTH the commit and the apply
  until the founder approves it. Rehearse on a throwaway copy with `prisma
  migrate deploy` (never `migrate dev`); measure `pg_class.relfilenode` before
  and after to prove the add was metadata-only; probe in transactions that are
  rolled back; drop the copy. The founder's go before `gather_dev` is touched
  (Stop Condition 7). The migration and the screens go to main in one pull
  request and deploy together (ruling 18); every other Zone 5 step stands.
  Ruling 57 approved the SQL; ruling 58 held the commit of the schema and
  migration until the S1.3 rehearsal was green, and ruling 59 released it
  (`298c9c1`); ruling 62 gave the go for the apply to local `gather_dev`
  (S1.5), `gather_dev` only.
- **The marker is never read by a decision to message, link or count.** A
  tripwire test pins it (Stage 1).
- **Zone 9 is read only** (ruling 30). The refusal reads `EmailOptOut` with
  one direct query from non-zone code (`updateExistingMember`, S2.3), because
  Zone 9's own readers are per event; it edits no Zone 9 file and imports
  nothing from one. Nothing here writes `EmailOptOut` or `EmailBlock`.
- **Zone 7 is untouched: STOP handling does not change** (ruling 50, B8). A
  STOP belongs to the phone number, not the person; a number that is changed
  has no STOP on it, and that is accepted. A test pins that nothing in Stage 2
  touches `SmsOptOut`, `Person.smsOptedOut` or `opt-out-service.ts` (test item
  20), and Stage 3 is held to the same rule.
- **Stage 3 needs no server change and no new API route** (ruling 44,
  confirmed by ruling 53: a new page is fine). If the Stage 3 plan finds
  otherwise, stop and tell the founder.
- **The held contact details are never used** until the host chooses message.
  See the plain statement above.
- **Words.** Every sentence a host will read is a W-line below, ruled by the
  founder (ruling 7). Any new sentence found while building stops the build for
  a ruling.
- **No new API route.** If one turns out to be needed, stop and ask.
- **Standing rule on tooling failures (ruling 74).** A tooling failure that
  fires none of the founder's stop conditions may be fixed and resumed, with
  the evidence kept, as in S1.7's chunk C. It is reported plainly each time.
  Any real stop condition still ends the run at once. This holds for the rest
  of this ticket.
- **No commit, push or merge without the founder's approval in the chat.**
- **Nothing sends for real; never connect to production; never print a
  secret.** The safety lines in the brief apply to every test and every stage.
- **Stage 3 does not start** until A5 is ruled and the plan has settled the
  points under "What the Stage 3 plan must settle" with the founder.

Stage 1 (in the one pull request):

      (ruling 23), confirmed again when the SQL is shown.

- [ ] `test:tweaks-guard` lists this ticket's Zone 5 change (ruling 72), as

Stage 2:
- [ ] A kid row offers "Decide later" beside the tick, only while the tick is
      off (ruling 20).
- [ ] Saving it through all three doors stores role CHILD and the marker "yes";

- [ ] Ticking to message stores GUEST and the marker "no". If both ever arrive,
      the tick wins and the marker is "no"; a save that omits the marker

- [ ] A decide-later child with a phone and an email is excluded on every real

      list changes; Send is not blocked by them.
- [ ] Step 3's row shows `talkLine`'s line first and W7 under it, in the same

- [ ] Settling a decide-later child to "don't message" in the household form

- [ ] Nothing else is blanked: a plain child, a child saved before this ticket

- [ ] Named risk 1 (rulings 33 and 34, A1 on any event): settling a

The single deploy (ruling 18):

### The plan's questions, as asked (Claude Code, 2026-11-02)

Kept here so the rulings below can be read against them. Each carried a
recommendation, given first.

- Q1. How to hold decide later. Recommended: B, a yes/no marker beside the
  role, role stays CHILD. Or A, a fifth role value. Or C, no database change
  (does not work).
- Q2. Yes/no (recommended), or a date ("undecided since").
- Q3. The number. Recommended: (a) not stored. Or (b) held on the row, or (c)
  held only in the browser.
- Q4. Sending. Recommended: warn in step 3. Or allow silently, or block.
- Q5. Where she settles it. Recommended: the household form plus a read-only
  line in step 3. Or add a one-tap "Don't message" on the existing channel
  route, or a one-tap for both.
- Q6. Settling to "message" after the press sends within minutes. Recommended:
  a confirm line before it saves.
- Q7. Form shape. Recommended: keep the tick and add a "Decide later" choice
  beside it. Or replace both with a three-way choice.
- Q8. Words. Every new sentence needs the founder's ruling; the plan drafts
  them. Caution: the product already has "decide by".
- Q9. The Zone 5 steps: SQL shown first, commit and apply held, rehearsal
  source, the founder's go for `gather_dev`.
- Q10. Deploy the migration alone first (recommended), or together. (Ruled
  "alone first" in ruling 8; superseded by ruling 18.)
- Q11. Use GTC-449 (recommended); is any untracked ticket above 440 hidden?
- Q12. Add a `test:child-assignment` script (recommended), or run by path.
- Q13. Land this ticket before the GTC-448 pick screen (recommended).

### RULINGS 2026-11-02

The founder's message, the rulings, verbatim. The ruling numbers are this
ticket's; each names the question it answers. Nothing else in the ticket
changes their meaning.

A:

> One added column, default no. Not a date, not a fifth role value.

Ruling 2 (Q3, the number, with the follow-up).
Q: Where does a decide-later child's number sit?
A:
> The child's number is held on the child's row. Follow-up ruling:
> carry the number across ONLY when she picks "decide later". If she picks
> "message", the number fills in and she ticks the box herself. If she
> picks "don't message", the number is left out (name only). Say plainly in
> the ticket that this holds a number Gather will not use, and that the
> marker never lets it be used.

Ruling 3 (Q4, sending).

>    deleted). No server involved. You confirm the app knows the

>    after the saved-draft check. If it points at a person no longer in
>    the list, the form ignores it quietly.

>    has details replaces that row's name and details.

already saved takes no pick, and her own row in host mode takes no pick.

from the ones put to him. Each answer is the option he chose, exactly as the
tool recorded it: the option's label, written by Claude Code. He typed no words
of his own for these three and added no notes, so they are not his sentences
in the way earlier rulings are. Approving the plan and approving the commit
are not rulings and are not recorded as ones.

Ruling 112 (the new test file and its script).
Q: The S2.6 entry names one file, the card, and puts its test in
`test:decide-later-preflight`. That test had no file and no `package.json`
line. Ruling 76 names it. May S2.6 create
`tests/decide-later-preflight-test.ts` and add its one script line to
`package.json`?
A:
> Yes, create both now (Recommended)

The option as put: "Ruling 76 names the script, and the S2.6 entry says the
test goes there. One new test file, one script line (plus a comma on the line
before). S2.5 later adds its checks to the same file." The other option was to
put the W8 checks in `test:decide-later-form` and leave `package.json` alone.

Ruling 113 (a flagged kid with no name).
Q: A saved kid is on Decide later but has no name stored (normally impossible:
the form stops a save with no name). The card would show the kid line as just
an icon. Should W8 still appear for that kid?
A:
> Yes, still show (decide later) (Recommended)

The option as put: "The words are a warning that Gather will not message that
kid. Hiding them for a nameless kid would hide the warning. No new sentence is
needed." The other option was to leave the words out when there is no name.

Ruling 114 (how W8 looks).
Q: No ruling says how W8 should look. The default is plain text straight after
the kid's name, in the same size and grey as the rest of that line, with no new
colours or classes. Is that right?
A:
> Plain, same style as the line (Recommended)

The option as put: "Smallest change, no new styling decision. The look is
unseen until your S2.9 browser walk, as with the S2.4 confirm line." The other
option was a distinct style (for example italic or a different shade), to be
named by him.

An approval the founder gave in the same session, recorded here and not
numbered: he approved the S2.6 plan, which said:

> The dashed "not saved yet" card is built from names only, so it never
> carries the flag. W8 will be built for saved cards only, guarded the way
> W9 is, so a future change to the typed card cannot make it appear there.

His other instructions (plan mode first, tests first and red first, a break
proof for every check that arrived green, the safe setup, staging and
stopping, and the staged numbers to be quoted before the commit) are carried
out in the S2.6 evidence.

### RULINGS ON THE PRE-FLIGHT LOOK, THE NAMELESS KID AND THE TEST, 2026-11-08

The founder's answers to the three questions put at the start of S2.5, in plan
mode. The numbering continues from ruling 114. The questions are worded here
from the ones put to the founder. Each answer is the option chosen, exactly as
the tool recorded it: the option's label, written by Claude Code. The founder
typed no words for these three and added no notes, so they are not the
founder's sentences in the way earlier rulings are. Approving the plan,
approving the edits and approving the commit are not rulings and are not
recorded as ones.

Ruling 115 (how W5 and W6 look).
Q: No ruling says how W5 ("Decide later: Josh, Mere") and W6 ("Gather won't
message them unless you change this in their household.") should look inside
an open household in pre-flight step 3. They sit just under the existing small
grey "Kids: Josh, Mere" line. Which look do you want?
A:
> Plain, same style as the Kids line (Recommended)

The option as put: "Two short lines in the Kids line's own small grey type, W5
then W6, no new colour or class. Smallest change, and the same call you made
for W8 in ruling 114. The look stays unseen until your S2.9 browser walk." The
other option was "Amber, like the row line": "Same amber as W7 and the "no one
to talk to" hint, so the warning stands out more. This is a new styling
decision, so you would need to confirm the exact size and weight."

Ruling 116 (a kid on decide later with no name).
Q: A kid saved as Decide later with no name stored is normally impossible,
because the form stops that save. But if one existed, W5 could not print their
name. In step 3 (W5, W6 and the W7 count), what should happen for that kid?
A:
> Count them and still warn (Recommended)

The option as put: "W7 counts them, W6 still shows for that household, and W5
lists only the named kids (left out if none are named). Same reasoning as your
ruling 113 on the card: hiding it would hide the warning. No new sentence is
made up." The other option was "Leave them out everywhere": "Treat them as the
Kids line already does (it drops a nameless kid). W5, W6 and W7 all ignore
them. One simple rule, but in that rare case the warning is hidden."

Ruling 117 (the database section of the new test file).
Q: Items 6 and 13 go in test:decide-later-preflight, which today is a pure
drawing test that touches no database. To prove the pre-flight route really
returns the marker for each member, the file needs a section that calls the
real route on the throwaway copy, using made-up rows it creates and deletes by
id (nothing sent, no key). May the file gain that section?
A:
> Yes, add a database section (Recommended)

The option as put: "Same file, same script, no new file. It writes rows only
on the throwaway copy and removes only its own, like the other three
decide-later scripts. It is the only way to prove the marker truly comes back
from the route." The other option was "No, keep the file pure": "The route
would be checked by reading its source text only. That cannot prove the value
comes back correctly, so a wrong read would only show up at your S2.9 walk."

Approvals the founder gave in the same session, recorded here and not
numbered. First, the plan, in these words, with four conditions:

> The S2.5 plan is approved as written, with four conditions.
>
> 1. The W6 sentence has "won't" in it. Tell me whether the ticket's W6
>    uses a straight or a curly apostrophe, and confirm the code uses the
>    same one before the words check goes green.
>
> 2. For the database section of decide-later-preflight: tell me before
>    building how it keeps invite events at 21 and outbound messages at 0
>    (or, if it must add rows, how it restores them). After the run,
>    report the throwaway copy's table counts against the start, and
>    confirm only the test's own rows were removed.
>
> 3. Do not change tweaks-d, tweaks-f or marker-tripwire until I say so.
>    Run each one red first, then show me the exact data lines you
>    propose to add and what each check still protects, and wait for my
>    word before editing.
>
> 4. For the old suites you run from the scratch clone, tell me for each
>    one whether the browser-layer cut matched exactly once. Name any
>    suite you leave for S2.9.

Second, after the exact data lines were shown, in these words:

> Approved: make the three edits exactly as shown.
>
> - marker-tripwire: the one added line.
> - tweaks-f: the six pairs appended to GTC422_EDITS, with your comment
>   saying the list now holds GTC-449's hunks too.
> - tweaks-d: the six pairs appended to PAGE_EDITS and the six to
>   STEP_EDITS.

That approval covers ruling 82's amendments of `tweaks-d` P1 and P2 and

Third, the founder approved the commit: "Approved. Commit the ten staged S2.5

The founder's other instructions (plan mode first, tests first and red first,

### Claude Code's readings of the rulings

Where a ruling leaves room, the plan read it one way and said so. Each is a
reading, not a decision until the founder rules. **All of them are now
answered:** R1, R3, R7 and R10 by the second set of rulings (15, 16, 17, 18),
and R2, R4, R5, R6, R8, R9, R11, R12, R13, R14 and R15 by the third (19 to 29),
every one as the plan recommended. The text of each is kept as asked, with the
recommendation first, so the rulings can be read against it.

**Answered by the second set of rulings**

- **R1 — answered by ruling 15.** "Number" means contact details: the number
  and the email, one rule for both.
- **R3 — answered by ruling 16.** Only decide-later children get the confirm
  line. A plain child ticked to message after the press gets none, as today.
  The gap is a later item (see Open items).
- **R7 — answered by ruling 17.** Settling a decide-later child to "don't
  message" in the household form blanks the held number and email.
- **R10 — answered by ruling 18.** One branch, one pull request and one deploy
  for Stages 1 and 2; Stage 3 gets its own later ones.

**Answered by the third set of rulings** (19 to 29; recommendation first, as
asked)

- **R2. What Stage 2's form does with details typed on a kid row that is not
  decide later and not ticked.** **Answered by ruling 19.** Recommendation:
  leave it as today. Stage 2
  blanks nothing except the settle in ruling 17; the "left out for don't
  message" rule of ruling 15 is built at pick time in Stage 3. Reading: the
  carry rule is a picking rule. Cost of leaving it: a plain child can still
  hold details if she types them, so ruling 17's "only a decide-later child
  holds contact details" is true of the flows this ticket builds and not of
  children saved before or typed that way. The alternative, blanking details
  on every don't-message kid at save, changes children already saved the next
  time their household is saved, which the safe default rules out.
- **R4. Tick on and "Decide later" together.** **Answered by ruling 20.**
  Recommendation: as read. The
  choice is offered only while the tick is off; if both ever arrive, the tick
  wins and the marker is saved "no"; an omitted value clears the marker.
  Alternative: the server refuses a save that sends both (more visible, but
  more code and a new sentence to word). Consequence of "omitted clears": a
  stale tab or an old draft turns a "later" into a plain don't-message child.
  The blanking is in the form, so the row then ends as a plain child still
  holding its details, the state of today's unticked kid.
- **R5. "Read-only line" (ruling 4).** **Answered by ruling 21.**
  Recommendation: confirm as read. No
  link and no button; the sentence W6, approved exactly (ruling 13), says
  where to change it. Reading: the wording half is already answered by W6's
  approval; the open half is whether "read-only" also rules out making the
  kids' names in the line a link, and I read it as ruling that out.
- **R6. "Warn in step 3" (ruling 3).** **Answered by ruling 22.**
  Recommendation: confirm as read. Before
  the press the warning is W5 and W6 in step 3's body and W7 on its row (ruling
  14). After the press the page is replaced by `AfterThePress`, which does not
  list them; the household form and the card marker (W8) are where she finds
  them. Leave `AfterThePress` alone in this ticket and list it as a later item.
  Alternative: a one-line note on `AfterThePress` (new words, a test, and no
  ruling asks for it).
- **R8. "Message" from the prompt (ruling 2).** **Answered by ruling 29: left
  for Stage 3; Stage 2 changes nothing for it.** Recommendation: no change in
  Stage 2; decide
  it with the prompt in Stage 3 (it is Stage 3 question 7). Reading: the
  details fill in and the tick stays off until she ticks it; if she saves
  without ticking, the row is a plain child holding details, today's unticked
  kid.
- **R9. The column name.** **Answered by ruling 23; settled again when the SQL
  is shown.** Recommendation: `messagingUndecided` on
  `PersonEvent`: yes/no, not empty, default no. It avoids a `decide` prefix on
  purpose, since the code already has `decideBy` names and the tripwire greps
  for the marker's name. Alternatives: `decideLater`, the host's word, which
  sits beside the `decideBy` names and would also match a search for them; or
  `messagingDeferred`. A rename after the migration is committed costs a
  second migration, so it is settled when the SQL is shown.
- **R11. Where `test:child-assignment` lands.** **Answered by ruling 24.**
  Recommendation: now that
  Stages 1 and 2 are one pull request, add the script as its own small commit
  at the start of the branch, so every gate in the pull request, Stage 1's
  included, runs the GTC-207 paired guard. Alternative: with Stage 2, as first
  read, which was to keep Stage 1 minimal because it deployed alone; that
  reason is gone. No cost difference.
- **R12. The confirm line is a screen courtesy.** **Answered by ruling 25: no
  server guard.** Recommendation: confirm as
  read. The save route is unchanged and the line is not a gate; sending to a
  settled child is governed by the same eligibility code as any adult.
  Alternative: a server guard that refuses decide-later to message after the
  press unless the request carries a confirmation. That is stronger against a
  stale tab, but it adds a field on three doors, a new refusal with new words,
  and tests.

**New readings, found while applying the second set of rulings** (answered by
rulings 26, 27 and 28)

- **R13. The blanking applies only to a row that was SAVED as decide later.**
  **Answered by ruling 26.** Recommendation: yes. The form tells by comparing
  the row with the saved
  household it opened with (matched by `personEventId`). A row switched off
  decide later in the same sitting, before it was ever saved as decide later,
  is not blanked, because the host may have typed those details herself.
  Alternative: blank whenever Decide later is switched off on any row holding
  details; simpler, but it can wipe what she typed a moment ago.
- **R14. The blanking writes to a person record that other events may share.**
  **Answered by ruling 27.** Recommendation: accept it, as the edit door
  already does for every edit, and pin the behaviour in a test whichever way
  it is ruled. Facts: a typed row is matched to a `Person` by address and
  first name, "on this event or another" (GTC-293, the ruled trade;
  `findOrCreateCapturedPerson` limits the match to the event only on sample
  events). `updateExistingMember` writes `Person.email` and
  `Person.phoneNumber` for that row. So blanking a child's details also blanks
  them for any other event row that shares the person, which as far as the
  code shows can include another host's. Alternatives: refuse to blank when
  the person has another event row (a new server check in a door); or give the
  row a fresh `Person` without details (touches the identity rule; large).
- **R15. Step 3's closed row has one line slot, and it is already used.**
  **Answered by ruling 28.** Recommendation: show both lines, one under the
  other, when both apply, the
  "no one to talk to" line first and W7 in the same amber. This widens
  `PreflightStep` from one line to a list, and the tests that pin GTC-377's
  row lines need extending. No new words. Alternative: W7 only when the first
  line has nothing to say, which hides a decide-later kid on a folded step 3
  whenever a household has no one to talk to.

### The plan, as ruled — Stages 1 and 2: one branch, one PR, one deploy

Ruling 18. Stages 1 and 2 are built on this branch, in order, and reach main
in ONE pull request and ONE deploy. Zone 5's holds stand on the migration
commit: SQL shown first, commit and apply held, rehearsal on a throwaway copy,
the founder's go before `gather_dev`. Stage 2's code commits come after the
apply to `gather_dev`, because the client must be regenerated against the
column. Nothing is pushed or merged until both stages are built and gated.

#### The start of the branch — the script (ruling 24)

- **S0 `test:child-assignment`.** Its own small commit at the start of the
  branch: the ticket's docs commit comes first, then S0, then Stage 1 (ruling
  32). It changes `package.json` only: one script line pointing at the
  existing `tests/child-assignment-eligibility-test.ts` (the paired guard for
  GTC-207, which has run only by hand and has never been under the "every
  `test:*` script" gate). It is not part of Zone 5; the migration commit is a
  different commit and stays held. It needs the founder's approval to commit,
  like every commit. Before the commit the test is run once on main, by path,
  to see that it is green on `128b341`, and S0 stops if it is red (ruling 46):
  a red script must not enter the gate unannounced. Every gate in the pull
  request, Stage 1's included, then runs it as a script. Undo: revert the one
  line.

#### Stage 1 — the marker and its migration

Goal: the column exists, unused, and a test guarantees nothing that sends,
links or counts will ever read it.

- **S1.1 Schema.** Add the marker to `model PersonEvent` in
  `prisma/schema.prisma` (`messagingUndecided`, ruled in ruling 23 and settled
  again when the SQL is shown: yes/no, not empty, default no) with a doc
  comment saying: only meaningful while `householdRole` is CHILD; never an
  input to messaging, links or counts; the held number it may sit beside is
  never used. Risk: a wrong name or type. Undo while uncommitted: revert the
  line.
- **S1.2 Generate, held.** `prisma migrate dev --create-only --skip-generate`.
  The migration is one "add column" statement, the same kind as the
  `isYoungPerson` one. Stop and show the founder the SQL. Hold the commit AND
  the apply. Undo while uncommitted: delete the migration folder.
- **S1.3 Rehearse.** On a throwaway copy (a dump clone, `--no-owner
  --no-acl`), apply with `prisma migrate deploy` only. Measure
  `pg_class.relfilenode` of `PersonEvent` before and after. Prove every
  existing row reads "no". Probe in rolled-back transactions. Drop the copy
  and prove `gather_dev` untouched. The copy comes from a fresh dump of the
  founder's local `gather_dev`, taken the way the earlier backup was (ruling
  47): GTC-448 recorded a `pg_dump` file in `~/gather-db-backups/`, restored
  to a throwaway database and checked with `pg_restore --list`. Never from
  production; this work never connects to it. Risk: that dump is not a copy of
  production, which is the standing expectation in the constants' Deploy
  caveat (C12), and a local Postgres may not be the production version; the
  metadata-only claim needs 11 or later. Checklist item 3 asks the founder to
  read the production version.
- **S1.4 The two extra rehearsal checks (ruling 18).** The screens now ship in
  the same deploy as the migration, so the throwaway copy also proves the
  pair. (a) The backward check: from a clean throwaway clone of `origin/main`
  `128b341`, made outside this folder, run the existing `test:*` set against
  the migrated copy. That is the code in production today running beside the
  new column, which is what a rollback returns to. (b) The forward check: once
  Stage 2 is built, run its whole test set against the same migrated copy
  (S2.9). Nothing sends; the copy is dropped afterwards.

  *Amended by ruling 60 (3 Nov 2026).* The words above stand as written.
  Ruling 60 makes the backward check, (a), option B, the targeted set, in place
  of the whole existing gate; the set is listed under "S1.4 — the targeted
  set". The full set still runs on the real branch at S1.7 and S2.9.
- **S1.5 Apply to `gather_dev`** only on the founder's go. Apply first,
  regenerate the client second (KB-005; the memory trap is `P2022` on a client
  generated ahead of the column). Undo: a second migration that drops the
  column.
- **S1.6 The tripwire test.** A new test, in the style of the existing layer-W
  and layer-N source scans: no file in `src/lib/eligibility`, `src/lib/press`,
  `src/lib/sms`, `src/lib/wrap-up.ts`, `src/lib/tokens.ts`, `src/lib/glance`,
  `src/lib/ai/plan-input.ts` or `src/lib/eligibility/shared-link-exposure.ts`
  names the marker. It gets an npm script. Written red-then-green by adding a
  deliberate mention in a scratch copy outside the repo.
- **S1.7 Stage 1 gate.** Typecheck, format check, `next build`, the full
  `test:*` set (which now includes `test:child-assignment`, from S0). Nothing
  sends. Stage 1 is not pushed or deployed on its own.

What could go wrong in Stage 1, and undo: a wrong name or type (revert while
uncommitted; once committed, a second migration); a brief lock on `PersonEvent`
during the add (deploy away from a press); a build that fails after the
migration (the old deployment keeps serving beside an unused column: harmless).
Removing the column is a second migration, never an edit of the first.

#### S1.4 — the targeted set (ruling 60)

Written before the run, on 3 Nov 2026, from GTC-448's record (the order,
result and time of each of its 164 scripts, and its nine known reds) and from
reading each test's source. Ruling 60 chose option B in place of the whole
existing gate; the full set still runs on the real branch at S1.7 and S2.9.

**How it runs.** From a clean clone of `128b341` in a new folder outside this
repo (`npm ci`, and the client generated from the clone's own old schema),
against a throwaway copy of `gather_dev` made from a fresh dump, with the
migration applied by `prisma migrate deploy` on the copy only. The guarded
setup of S0: Node 22 through the guard stand-in, the OS wall, provider keys
blank, made-up Stripe values, `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS` never set, and
`GATHER_DEMO_START=on` and a made-up `UNSUBSCRIBE_TOKEN_SECRET` as GTC-448's
run 5 had them. The clone's `.env` is a sanitised copy that points at the copy
of the database, holds no real key and a made-up cron secret; there is no
`.env.local`. One script at a time. Before every script the runner checks, and
prints only yes or no and the database name, that the connection points at the
copy. After every script it checks InviteEvent|OutboundMessage on the copy
(baseline 21|0), the list of databases, and swap.

**Stops.** Swap used above 10.8 of 11.3 GB (the founder will not close
Chrome): stop and wait. Any red that is not one of the known reds below, or a
known red whose failing checks are not the recorded ones: stop at once and
report the first failing assertion; change nothing. A known red is recorded
and the run continues. A real send (OutboundMessage above 0, InviteEvent above
21) or a guard refusal of a provider host: stop.

**How the scripts were chosen.** GTC-448 counted 37 database-only scripts but
did not list them. The plan found candidates by reading imports: a test whose
imports reach Prisma, that starts no server and drives no browser. There are
64, a superset of the 37, because some import database code and never call it
(GTC-448 saw them green without a database). All 64 run, with the ticket's
other named scripts that need no database or need a server.

**Left out, said plainly.** The 30 browser scripts, including
`test:household-save`, which the ticket names under household save and edit:
it drives headless Chrome, which cannot run inside the wall and needs Chrome
open. It is Stage 2's own test and runs at S1.7 and S2.9. Also left out: the
other server scripts the ticket does not name, the scripts that need neither
database nor server, and `test:gtc280-paid` (red by design; it waits on
`GTC280_PAID_SESSION_ID`).

**The known reds inside this set** (three of GTC-448's nine; the other six are
browser or server scripts outside it, so none of them runs here):

- `test:security`: 243 of 253, ten red, the same ten every time in GTC-448.
  Six in suite 11, because the demo event is named "...2026" and the route
  wants "...2025": the anonymous demo-session control; the minted session
  reaches exactly one event, the demo event; every event it reaches is a demo
  event; it expires in hours; it belongs to a dedicated user; a same-named
  non-demo event does not hide the demo one. Four live controls, because the
  suite reads the cron secret only from `.env.local` and there is none: the
  `?secret=` and Bearer positive controls, and the two LEAK [live] body checks
  for `wrap-up-dispatch` and `decide-by-followups`.
- `test:demo-endpoints`: 5 of 6. The one red is the check
  `Demo event "Henderson Family Christmas 2025" exists in DB` (the same name
  drift).
- `test:demo-tweaks-5`: 36 of 39. The three red are B5, U1 and U2 (the git tag
  `demo-tweaks-4-good` is absent here and on origin).

**The set.** Forecasts are GTC-448's recorded results at `f38ba0b` (the same
code as `128b341` apart from its docs); a count that differs with exit 0 is
noted, not a red.
