#### Stage 2 — form, blanking, confirm line, pre-flight lines, card

Goal: she can choose "Decide later" for a kid, it is saved, it is shown in
pre-flight and on the card, and a plain child, an adult and every send path
behave exactly as before.

- **S2.1 The write path, three doors.** `createMember` in
  `households/route.ts`, `reconcileHouseholdMembers` (both
  `updateExistingMember` and `createNewMember`, which write the role) and
  `host-household/route.ts` take an optional decide-later input on kid rows.
  The marker is written "yes" only for a kid row with the tick off and decide
  later on; the tick wins; an omitted value clears it (ruling 20). The server
  tells an explicit `false` from an omitted value, and only an explicit
  `false` can be read as a settle (S2.3); `LoadedMember` gains the saved
  marker. No gate file is touched. Nothing else is blanked or changed on a
  plain kid row, whatever it holds (ruling 19). Risk: a stale browser tab
  saves without the field and loses a "later" (it falls to "don't message",
  the safe direction, and keeps its details). Undo: revert; the column stays,
  unread.
- **S2.2 The form.** In `Moment1InputForm.tsx`, a "Decide later" choice beside
  the tick, offered only while the tick is off (ruling 20). Carry it through
  `form-draft.ts` (`isRow` and the restore), `apiHouseholdToSaved` in
  `setup/page.tsx` (rebuild from the saved marker),
  `household-channel-choices.ts` (the type; a decide-later kid stays out of
  the contact picker because the tick is off) and the payload builders. Risk:
  a draft saved before the change restores without the field and becomes a
  plain child (safe). Undo: revert.
- **S2.3 The settle: the blanking (ruling 17), the refusal (ruling 33), the
  door and the part of the form.** *The part of the form:* the kid row's
  "Decide later" choice in
  `Moment1InputForm.tsx`. When she turns it off on a row that was saved as
  decide later, with the tick still off (that is "don't message"), the
  choice's change handler empties that row's email and phone boxes in the
  form's state. They show blank at once; nothing is stored until she presses
  Save, and Cancel or Discard leaves the stored details alone. The form knows
  the row "was saved as decide later" by comparing it with the saved household
  it opened with, matched by `personEventId` (ruling 26); a row with no
  `personEventId`, or one switched off in the same sitting before it was ever
  saved as decide later, is not blanked. *The save door:* the edit door only.
  The blank boxes ride the existing payload to `PUT households/[householdId]`,
  which calls `reconcileHouseholdMembers`; `updateExistingMember` writes
  `Person.email` and `Person.phoneNumber` as null when a row arrives blank.
  Nothing else can settle: the new-household door (`POST households`) and her
  own household's first save (`createHostHousehold`, which its own header calls
  "a create path for new events only" and which hands reconcile only her own
  membership as existing) create rows that have never been saved as decide
  later. Her later edits and settles go through the same `PUT` as every other
  household (C11). Settling to "message" blanks nothing: the details are the
  adult's now.
  - *How the server tells a settle from an ordinary clear (ruling 33).* In
    `updateExistingMember`, a request is a settle only if all four hold: (1)
    the saved marker on the row is "yes", read from the household the `PUT`
    loads in the same transaction; (2) the payload carries `decideLater:
    false` explicitly, not omitted; (3) the tick is off, so the role is CHILD;
    (4) the email arrives blank. That is the saved marker going from yes to no
    with the tick off and blank details arriving together, plus one addition:
    (2) asks for an explicit false so that a request which merely leaves the
    marker out is not read as a settle.
  - *What the refusal does.* For a settle, if the person has an `EmailOptOut`
    row on ANY event (rulings 33 and 34), `updateExistingMember` leaves
    `email` out of the person update, so the email stays on the record. The
    phone, the name and the rest are written as sent; the phone is not at risk
    because its opt-out is on the number. The role and the marker are set as
    for any settle.
  - *How the any-event read is done, from non-zone code (ruling 34).* Zone 9's
    own readers are per event: `getEmailOptOut(personId, eventId)` and
    `listEmailOptOutsForEvent(db, eventId)`, both in `email-opt-out.ts`.
    Neither can ask "any event", and the task does not allow a Zone 9 edit to
    add one. So the read is **one direct query, written inside
    `updateExistingMember` in `reconcileMembers.ts`**:
    `tx.emailOptOut.findFirst({ where: { personId }, select: { id: true } })`,
    a read only, answered by "is there a row". `tx` is the transaction client
    that function already holds: `reconcileHouseholdMembers` takes its
    `prisma` parameter as `Prisma.TransactionClient`, and the `PUT` calls it
    inside `prisma.$transaction`. The query is by the person only, so it uses
    the leading column of the existing `@@unique([personId, eventId])` index.
    It runs only when a settle with a blank email is met, once per such row.
    It imports nothing from `email-opt-out.ts` or any Zone 9 file; direct
    reads of this table from non-zone code have precedent (`glance/rewind.ts`,
    and the unsubscribe page). No Zone 9 file is edited: `reconcileMembers.ts`
    is not one, and `prisma/schema.prisma` is edited in `model PersonEvent`
    only, never in `EmailOptOut` or `EmailBlock`. The trade-off, said once:
    this is a second spelling of "has this person opted out" outside Zone 9's
    module. It is not a gate. It decides whether a data edit keeps an email,
    it cannot send, link or count anything, and it can only keep an email,
    never clear one.
  - *Everything else is as today (ruling 33).* A stale or old tab that omits
    the marker: the marker is cleared (ruling 20), nothing is read as a
    settle, and the email is written as sent. A request that blanks the email
    without changing the marker (a plain row, no to no; or a decide-later row
    staying yes): the email is cleared even if an `EmailOptOut` row exists,
    which is today's ordinary clear kept exactly, and a later item. The tick
    on (message): not a settle. A row with no `personEventId`: not a settle.
  - *Reliable without a new field or a new route?* Yes. It uses the saved
    marker that the `PUT` already loads and the `decideLater` field that S2.1
    adds. If either were missing, the rule falls back to "not a settle", which
    is today's behaviour: it fails toward "as today", never toward refusing
    something that should not be refused. The forged case (an explicit false
    on a saved-yes row with a blank email) only keeps an email, which is
    harmless.
  - *What the form shows after a refusal.* After a good `PUT`,
    `handleEditSave` in `setup/page.tsx` rebuilds the household from the
    response and closes the form, so the saved card shows what is stored: the
    email kept, the phone blank. To tell her, the page compares what it sent
    with what came back: a kid row saved as decide later, sent with decide
    later off and a blank email, whose email comes back non-empty. Then it
    shows W9 as a quiet line on that household's card until she next opens the
    household or leaves the page. No new server field. A reload, or reopening
    the household, shows the email again, because it is on the record; the
    line is gone, and the kid is a plain don't-message row. W9, as ruled
    (ruling 35), has no second line for the phone.

Risks: the row after the refusal is a plain row, so a second save that blanks
its email is today's ordinary clear and is not refused (the later item); the
person record may be shared with other events, which can reach another host's
guest list (ruling 27, accepted); an old tab has no Decide later choice, so it
cannot settle. Undo: Cancel or Discard before Save; after Save the cleared
details are gone and she types them again. **Order:** Named risk 1 is ruled
(rulings 33 and 34) and W9 is ruled (ruling 35), so S2.3 is built to them and
waits for nothing.
- **S2.4 The confirm line (ruling 16).** When the event has already been
  pressed and a decide-later child is being saved to "message", the form shows
  the confirm line (W4) before it saves. Only a decide-later child; a plain
  child gets none, as today. A screen courtesy only: the save route is
  unchanged and there is no server guard (ruling 25).
- **S2.5 Pre-flight.** The pre-flight route returns the marker with each
  member. Step 3 shows a read-only line (W5, W6) per household that has a
  decide-later kid, beside the "Kids:" line, from a small pure function next
  to `kidsNote`. It has no link and no button, and the kids' names in it are
  not links (ruling 21). Step 3's row shows both of its lines (ruling 28): the
  existing `talkLine` line first and W7 under it, in the same amber.
  `PreflightStep` takes one line today (`line?: StepLine | null`), so it is
  widened to take two. The row line is drawn in the header whether the step is
  folded or open (C9), so W7 shows on the row either way; it is the folded row
  that needs it, since W5 and W6 sit in the body. The marker joins step 3's
  fingerprint in `preflightPrints` so a change elsewhere un-ticks the step.
  Send is not blocked by any of it (ruling 3). `AfterThePress` is left alone
  and is a later item (ruling 22). Tests that pin the old one-line shape are
  extended with this step (see "The tests"). Risk: a stale tick; a test that
  pins the old shape. Undo: revert.
- **S2.6 The card marker (ruling 14).** W8 on the saved household card in
  `HouseholdCardList.tsx`, beside the kid's name. Display only. Undo: revert.
- **S2.7 (moved).** The `test:child-assignment` script is S0, the first commit
  of the branch (ruling 24). The number is kept so the other step numbers hold.
- **S2.8 Tests**, written red-then-green alongside S2.1 to S2.6, including the
  blanking checks: see "The tests".
- **S2.9 Gate and the forward check.** The full `test:*` set, typecheck, format
  check, `next build`, a real browser walk of the form and pre-flight (nothing
  sends). Then Stage 2's test set against the migrated throwaway copy (S1.4b).
- **S2.10 Land and the one deploy (ruling 18).** Commit, push, ONE pull
  request, merge: each only on the founder's approval. The founder presses
  Railway's deploy once, away from a press, and confirms in the build log the
  live-sends line, the migration applied and a clean build. Then he walks one
  household save and the pre-flight page himself, with nothing sent. **Before
  anyone presses deploy he confirms the two things on the Railway checklist
  (ruling 48):** which branch Railway builds, and whether a redeploy of the
  last good version re-runs the build. The pull request merges with a merge
  commit, as pull requests 78 to 84 did (ruling 49). Stage 3 gets its own
  later branch and pull request.

The limit, said once: after the press the pre-flight is replaced by
`AfterThePress`, so the read-only lines are a before-the-press warning. After
the press the household form and the card marker are where she finds and
settles a decide-later child.

#### The one deploy: what it risks that two deploys would not, and the undo

Said plainly, as ruling 18 asks.

What is the same as with two deploys. The build script runs `prisma migrate
deploy` before `next build` in the same build, so the new code is never built
against a database without the column; if the migration fails, the build stops
and the old deployment keeps serving. The change is additive, so the old code
keeps working beside the column while the build runs.

What one deploy risks that two would not:

1. **No proof of the migration alone in production.** The first of two deploys
   would show, in its build log and in the running app, that the column went on
   cleanly (no lock trouble, no surprise from real data) before any screen read
   it. With one deploy, if something is wrong afterwards, the migration and the
   screens cannot be told apart.
2. **The household save changes in the same press as the schema.** The three
   save doors and the form are the host's capture flow. A fault there reaches
   hosts at once. With two deploys the first would already be proven and the
   second would carry the fault alone.
3. **The rollback target has never run beside the column in production.**
   Undoing the single deploy returns to the code from before this ticket. That
   is safe on paper: the old code ignores the column, and rows marked "yes" are
   still CHILD. But two deploys would have given a middle rung, Stage 1's code,
   proven in production with the column present. S1.4(a) stands in for it on a
   copy; it is not production.
4. **One pull request holds both.** If Stage 2 needs more work, the migration
   waits on the branch with it and cannot be merged ahead. Merging to main does
   not deploy (auto-deploy is off), so waiting is harmless; but a manual deploy
   of main at any moment takes both together.

The same either way: a brief lock on `PersonEvent` while the column is added; a
build that fails after the migration applied (the old deployment keeps serving,
and the column is already there); a stale browser tab, which saves without the
marker and so clears it (the safe direction).

What reduces it (the plan's proposal, not a ruling): S1.4's two checks on the
throwaway copy; deploying away from a press; the founder's own walk after.

How to undo it:

- **The code.** The founder goes back to the last good deployment from
  Railway's Deployments tab, by hand. Railway's documentation (read 2 Nov
  2026; see the Railway checklist) says a **Rollback** reuses that
  deployment's stored image and variables with no rebuild, so no `migrate
  deploy` runs, and that it works only while Railway still holds the image (24
  to 360 hours, by plan). A **Redeploy** runs the build again from the same
  source, which includes `migrate deploy`; if that is from an older commit,
  S1.4(a) shows what `migrate deploy` does with a migration the older code
  does not have. He confirms this in his own dashboard before the press
  (ruling 48). After a Rollback he re-reads `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS`, since no
  build prints the check.
- **The data.** Nothing to undo. The column is "no" by default. Rows saved
  "yes" stay CHILD, so they are never messaged, by the old code or the new;
  their held details stay on the rows, unused, until the code returns. A child
  settled to "message" is a GUEST row, as the tick makes one today.
- **The column.** Left in place. Removing it is a second migration in a later
  deploy, only if wanted, and never an edit of the committed migration (Zone
  5).
- **A failed build.** Nothing new is served; the old deployment continues.
- **What a rollback cannot restore.** Details blanked by the host's own save
  when she settled a child to "don't message" (ruling 17). Nothing in the code
  can put them back.

### The Railway checklist (ruling 48) — for the founder to confirm first

Two things the founder confirms before anyone presses deploy, and two the plan
adds for his word. Written 2 Nov 2026 from Railway's public documentation (the
guide "Roll back a bad deploy"), not from the dashboard: nothing here connects
to Railway or to production. He confirms each in his own dashboard.

1. **Which branch does Railway build?** Open the service's Settings, then
   Source, and read the connected branch. It must say `main`. The upstream
   `master` no longer exists (C1, C2), and auto-deploy has been off since
   2026-08-10, so a manual deploy builds whatever branch is connected. Write it
   down: "branch = ____".
2. **Does redeploying the last good version re-run the build?** It depends on
   which button. Railway's documentation says: *Rollback* "restores a previous
   deployment's image and variables as a new active deployment" and does not
   rebuild ("there is no rebuild"). *Redeploy* "creates a new deployment from
   the same source, which includes a build". Our build is `npm run build`,
   which includes `prisma migrate deploy`, so a Rollback runs no migration
   step and a Redeploy does. Rollback only works while Railway still holds the
   old image: 24 hours on Free or Trial, 72 on Hobby, 120 on Pro, 360 on
   Enterprise. Confirm on the Deployments tab that the last good deployment
   offers Rollback, and which plan he is on. Write it down: "plan = ____;
   rollback offered: yes / no".
3. **(Added by the plan; strike it if you disagree.) The production Postgres
   version.** The rehearsal copy comes from local `gather_dev` (ruling 47), so
   it cannot show the production server's version. "Add a column with a
   default is metadata only" needs Postgres 11 or later. Read the version
   number from the Railway Postgres service. A number, not data. Write it
   down: "version = ____".
4. **(Added by the plan.) After a Rollback, re-read `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS`.** A
   Rollback restores the earlier deployment's variables too, and the
   live-sends check line is printed only by a build, which a Rollback does not
   run. So read the variable; do not rely on a build log.

### The plan, as ruled — Stage 3: contacts page, picking, prompt (outline)

Not planned. This is an outline: it gathers what the rulings and the founder's
run sheet already fix, and nothing more. **A5, the wording for how Gather
treats contacts, is not ruled yet.** Stage 3 has its own later branch and pull
request (ruling 18). Its branch name is proposed with the Stage 3 plan, and it
merges with a merge commit (ruling 49).

#### The run sheet's Stage 3 steps, as the founder gave them (verbatim)

The founder's words: *"You said you could not see my run sheet, so here are its
Stage 3 steps as they stand. Fold them into the Stage 3 outline, with my design
points above. This is still an outline, not a plan. A5, the wording for how
Gather treats contacts, is not ruled yet."*

> - C1. Give contacts a screen of their own, reachable from anywhere.
>   Not a pop-up or an overlay. The household list keeps an entry
>   point beside Add a household: large in the empty state, smaller
>   once households exist. Today the import button is on the older
>   dashboard only, about four clicks from Moment 1. Decided (A13): a
>   page of its own.
> - C2. Show the loaded list as it grows, with problems flagged on
>   each row. A row with a problem says Gather will take care of it
>   and offers three ways forward: double-check it, skip it, or use a
>   different way. A person with no number or email still loads and
>   is still a guest. No ticking who is coming, since the host places
>   people later.
> - C3. Add paste-a-list. A box that takes names with phone numbers or
>   emails, including text copied from a spreadsheet or a group chat.
>   One person per line, name then phone then email, separated by
>   commas. The reading part is built (GTC-448). The box itself and
>   the screen it sits on are still to do.
> - C4. Add the phone's own contact picker, only where it exists
>   (Chrome on Android). It must quietly stay out of the way on iPhone
>   and laptops.
> - C5. Keep file upload as the fallback. CSV and vCard, already
>   built. Move it off the older dashboard and read the file in the
>   browser.
> - C6. Add a short help note for exporting contacts from a laptop,
>   for iPhone hosts.
> - C7. Keep the loaded list in the browser only, with a live count
>   and delete. Nothing is sent to Gather until a person is added to a
>   household. A count of what is held is always on screen and goes
>   down as she deletes. She can delete one person or all of them.
>   Its own key prefix, so the existing sweep that clears household
>   drafts never touches the contacts. Nothing wipes the list on
>   sign-out or when an event is sent; only her Clear button, or
>   deleting a person, removes them.
> - D8. Let the host pick a loaded person from the name fields on the
>   household form by typing the first few letters of any word in a
>   name.
> - D9. Fill the right fields when someone is picked: number and
>   email plus the name; for children, the name only (with the decide
>   later number rule). Never tick Old enough to message directly.
>   Fill only on an explicit pick, and let the host edit afterwards.
> - D10. Search only the host's own loaded list. Never search the main
>   person table.
> - D11. Make the dropdown easy to read. Match the start of any word,
>   ignoring capitals. Names first, then the email or phone beside
>   them. Grey out people already placed in a household.
> - D12. Make it work on a phone. The dropdown must not hide behind
>   the keyboard, and picking must be one tap.
> - D13. Show where each loaded person stands: loaded but not placed,
>   placed in a household, and what will happen about messaging. The
>   host assigns people herself, and nobody is contacted until she
>   has. Placed means matching held contacts to saved members by
>   email; someone with no email cannot be matched reliably, so
>   recording each placement in the browser at save time may be
>   safer. Badges are per event.
> - D14. Alert the host when a child's details arrive with a number.
>   The options are message, don't message and decide later.

#### How the pieces fit (outline only)

The contacts page and the list (C1 to C7; A13 and A7 in ruling 12; rulings 36,
38, 44, 45):
- A page of its own, not a pop-up or overlay, reachable from anywhere, with an
  entry point beside Add a household on the household list.
- Ways to load: paste a list (C3), the phone's own contact picker where it
  exists (C4), CSV and vCard upload read in the browser (C5), and a help note
  for laptop exports (C6). The reading code is GTC-448's, already merged.
- The list grows with problems flagged on each row (C2), a live count that is
  always on screen, delete one or delete all (C7).
- Kept per person, in her browser, under her sign-in; hidden from anyone else
  who signs in on the device, not deleted; its own key prefix; nothing wipes
  it on sign-out or when an event is sent. Only her Clear button, or deleting
  a person, removes them, and Clear drops the hand-off note too (rulings 36
  and 38).
- No server (ruling 44).

Picking (D8 to D12; A8 and A11 in ruling 12; rulings 37, 39, 41, 42, 51):
- A pick is made from a name field on the household form: main contact,
  partner, guests and kids with jobs (ruling 39), on a new or unsaved row only
  (ruling 51).
- A row already saved takes no pick. To use a loaded contact in place of a
  saved member, she removes that member and adds a new row. Her own row in host
  mode, the main contact on her own household, takes no pick (ruling 51).
- It searches only her own loaded list, by the start of any word, ignoring
  capitals, names first and the email or phone beside them (D10, D11). It
  fills only on an explicit pick and she can edit afterwards (D9). It never
  ticks "Old enough to message directly".
- Adults get name, email and phone. A child gets the name only, with the
  decide-later rule of rulings 2 and 15 (D9, A8).
- A flagged number is carried as it is, the list shows the load-time flag, and
  the form's own check at save refuses it (ruling 41). A person with no number
  or email can be picked as a name only (ruling 42).
- The hand-off is a short-lived note in per-tab storage, used once and removed,
  applied after the saved-draft check; a note that points at a person no longer
  in the list is ignored quietly (ruling 37; A13 in ruling 12).

Placement and the prompt (D13, D14; A11; rulings 40, 43 and 52):
- Each loaded person shows where they stand: loaded but not placed, placed in a
  household, and what will happen about messaging. Badges are per event (D13).
- A contact already placed in another household on this event is greyed out
  and cannot be picked again, with a plain note saying which household (ruling
  40, D11).
- For a child whose details arrive with a number or email, the prompt offers
  message, don't message and decide later (D14, A11). Message: the details
  fill in and she ticks the box herself. Don't message: name only. Decide
  later: the details are carried and Stage 2's marker is set (rulings 2 and
  15).
- The prompt writes into the form's kid-row state and never sets a role itself
  (C5).
- A kid row that holds details with the tick off, and not on decide later,
  gets a one-line warning on save (ruling 43), narrowed by ruling 52: only when
  the details came from a pick this sitting. It informs and does not stop the
  save. Children saved before this ticket and kids typed with the tick off
  never trigger it. Its words are a W-line for the founder's ruling.

What stays out (ruling 50; ruling 44): STOP handling. Nothing in Stage 2 or
Stage 3 reads or writes `SmsOptOut`, `Person.smsOptedOut` or
`opt-out-service.ts`. A STOP belongs to the phone number; if a household's
number is changed, the new number has no STOP on it, and that is accepted.

#### What the code already gives Stage 3 (read 2 Nov 2026 at `128b341`)

This answers ruling 36's confirmation and ruling 44's check.

- **The browser knows who is signed in.** The root layout
  (`src/app/layout.tsx`) reads the session on the server (`getUser` in
  `src/lib/auth/session.ts`) and hands `user.id` to `Providers`, whose
  `ViewerIdContext` carries it to every page; it is null when nobody is signed
  in. Nothing is missing for a per-person key. It is the User's id, not the
  Person's. Signed out, the id is null and the list is not shown; it stays in
  storage and reappears when she signs in again.
- **Why "hidden, not deleted" is a different rule from the household drafts.**
  `sweepDrafts`, run by `Providers` on every page load, deletes every
  household draft that is not the signed-in person's, and all of them when
  nobody is signed in. It only scans keys that start `gather:household-draft:`
  (`DRAFT_PREFIX`), so a separate prefix is never touched, as C7 asks. Said
  plainly: "hidden" means hidden from Gather's screens. It is not protected
  from someone using the same browser profile signed in as her, or the
  browser's own tools; that is what A7 chose.
- **Per-tab storage already has a precedent.** `plan/new/page.tsx` keeps
  `gather_new_event` in `sessionStorage`. It is per tab and goes when the tab
  closes. A new tab does not inherit it, so the hand-off must be a same-tab
  navigation.
- **The saved-draft check can be waited for.** `Moment1InputForm` looks for a
  draft once per opening, in an effect that sets `draftChecked` when it is
  done. A hand-off applied after `draftChecked` equals the form is after the
  check.
- **The form's save check already refuses what ruling 41 leaves to it.**
  `validateEmail` and `validatePhone` run at save for every row, kids
  included: an overseas or malformed number is refused with the form's own
  words, and a named row needs its name. (If a request skipped the form, the
  server would drop an unusable number silently; a pick always goes through
  the form.)
- **Placements can be checked against what is saved.** `GET
  /api/events/[id]/households` already returns the saved households, and the
  setup page uses it.
- **No server change and no new API route is needed.** A new page is a new
  route in Next's sense, but the middleware matches only `/p/`, `/h/` and
  `/c/` pages and the `/api/p/` and `/api/c/` prefixes, so a page elsewhere
  needs no change (Zone 1 untouched); and the security route scan walks
  `src/app/api` only, with all 86 classified routes under it, so a page needs
  no classification (Zone 6 untouched). Ruling 53 confirms that ruling 44 means
  no new API route and that a new page is fine, since A13 and C1 give the
  contacts screen a page of its own (C13).

#### What the Stage 3 plan must settle (not decided here)

The rulings leave these open. Each is the plan's to put to the founder with a
recommendation. Items 3, 4 and 5 are ruled (rulings 52 and 51); they stay in
the list so the numbering reads, with what each ruling decided and what is
left.

1. **A5**, the wording for how Gather treats contacts. Not ruled.
2. **What "placed" means (D13).** Matching held contacts to saved members by
   email, or recording each placement in the browser at save time. The run
   sheet says the second "may be safer", because someone with no email cannot
   be matched. The greying of ruling 40 and the per-event badges both depend
   on it. Ruling 51 answers this only as far as it goes: a pick fills only a
   new or unsaved row, so a saved member is never re-pointed at another
   contact. Which mechanism reads "placed" stays on this list (the founder's
   note on clash 7).
3. **The trigger of ruling 43's warning: ruled (ruling 52).** As worded,
   ruling 43's warning also covered children saved before this ticket, and kids
   typed with the tick off, which ruling 19 leaves holding details, so every
   save of such a household would have warned. Ruling 52 rules: the warning
   shows only when the details came from a pick this sitting. It informs and
   does not stop the save. Children saved before this ticket and kids typed
   with the tick off never trigger it. Left for the plan: what "this sitting"
   means (one opening of the form, or whether a restored draft counts), whether
   editing or emptying the picked details after the pick removes the warning,
   and its words (a W-line, item 8).
4. **A pick into a saved row: ruled (ruling 51).** Ruling 39's "replaces that
   row's name and details" would have replaced the person behind an existing
   membership: the edit door updates that person in place, so the row's items,
   links, RSVP and nudge history would have belonged to a different human, on a
   person record that other events may share (ruling 27). Ruling 51 rules: a
   pick fills only new or unsaved rows, and a row already saved takes no pick.
   To use a loaded contact in place of a saved member, she removes that member
   and adds a new row. Left for the plan: how the form tells her a saved row
   takes no pick (the words, a W-line, item 8).
5. **A pick into her own household's main contact: ruled (ruling 51).** In host
   mode that row is herself: the form fills it from her account and keeps her
   email read-only. Ruling 51 rules: her own row in host mode (the main contact
   on her own household) takes no pick, so a pick cannot replace her own name
   and phone on her own person record. Left for the plan: nothing but the
   words, if the form says so (item 8).
6. **The page's address, and the way there and back.** Where it lives, how she
   reaches it from the household form and returns to the same form without
   losing her draft, the same-tab hand-off, how short "short-lived" is, the
   key prefix, a size cap, and what she is told when the browser refuses
   storage.
7. **The phone's contact picker (C4).** Where it exists, how it feeds the same
   readers, and staying out of the way elsewhere.
8. **The W-lines (ruling 45).** Drafted at plan time: the note for ruling 40,
   the warning for ruling 43 as narrowed by ruling 52 (its words stay a W-line
   for the founder's ruling), the Clear button and whether it asks first, the
   left-out counts, C2's three-ways-forward row, C6's help note and the live
   count, and, if the form says why a saved row or her own row takes no pick,
   that sentence (ruling 51).
9. **Stage 3's tests**, and its branch name (ruling 49). Rulings 51 and 52 fix
   some of the tests; they are named under "The tests".

the old numbering still reads.)

- 3, B8 (STOP when a number is saved or changed): ruling 50. No change.
- 4, where the list lives and who can see it: ruling 36.

- 7, the "message" pick that is never ticked (R8): ruling 43, narrowed by

- 8, a person already placed, or picked twice: ruling 40.
- 9, overseas or malformed numbers on pick: ruling 41.

- 12, whether Clear drops the hand-off id: ruling 38.
- 13, whether Stage 3 needs a server: ruling 44, confirmed by reading and by

- 14, R1 and R7: answered earlier (rulings 15 and 17).

### The Stage 2 plan for a new session — PROPOSED, NOT YET RULED

**Status, 3 Nov 2026.** Of the five questions at the end of this section:
the order is ruled by ruling 90; the pins by rulings 82 to 84 (Pins 5 and 6
need no amendment, F4 and F5); how S2.4 knows the invitations have gone by
ruling 91; how the work is committed by rulings 88 and 92. Only the go for
chore 16 is left, asked for at S2.9 (ruling 77). Ruling 93 sets how the checks
of F5 are run, and ruling 89 replaces W4's words. The text below is left as
filed.

**Build status, 8 Nov 2026.** Built and committed on the branch, in the
approved order (ruling 90): S2.1 (`e923e25`), the server half of S2.3
(`26c7bea`), S2.2 (`9a6e40f`), the form half of S2.3 (`731b8f5`), S2.4
(`87f9c3a`), S2.6 (`a98377c`) and S2.5 (`7afdf92`). Next are S2.8, S2.9 and
S2.10. The evidence for each is under Stage 2 in the Evidence section. The text
above and below is left as filed.

**Proposed, not yet ruled.** Written on 3 Nov 2026 at the end of the first
session, so that a new Claude Code session with no memory of it can carry out
Stage 2 from this ticket alone. Rulings 75 to 81 approve it in principle; the
final order is for the founder to read here before any building starts (ruling
75). Nothing below is built. This section adds to the older "Stage 2" plan
above (S2.1 to S2.10, which holds the detailed design of each step) and does
not replace it.

**Where things stand.** Branch `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only, never
pushed; `git log --oneline` shows one commit per finished piece. Stage 1 is
built and gated: the `PersonEvent.messagingUndecided` column (yes/no, not
empty, default no) is in the schema and in one migration, applied to local
`gather_dev` and `gather_demo` (59 migrations each, every existing row reads
no); S1.7 ran 166 scripts: 156 green and GTC-448's nine known reds
(`security`, `demo-ui`, `demo-endpoints`, `demo-sandbox`, `demo-tweaks-5`,
`cron-health`, `tnz-status-poll`, `phone-menu`, `preflight-fold`); the one new
red, `tweaks-guard`, was amended under ruling 72. Two guards now stop stray
edits: `test:marker-tripwire` (no sending, link or counting file may name the
marker) and `test:tweaks-guard` (the protected zones are held byte for byte).
Nothing in Stage 2 may edit a gate file, a Zone 1 to 9 file, or the schema.

**How the new session starts.** Read this ticket first (the parts named at the
end of this section), then do S2.0 only, the census, which is read only.
Report in the founder's format and stop. Do not start S2.1 on a "report"
alone.

**The proposed order.** S2.0, S2.1, S2.3 (server half), S2.2, S2.3 (form half),
S2.4, S2.6, S2.5, S2.8 (woven through), S2.9, S2.10. S2.7 moved to S0. Server
first, because the safety-critical part is the save doors and the settle rule;
the pre-flight step is last among the screens because it widens `PreflightStep`
and trips the most pinned tests. One new test file and one script per area
(ruling 76), appended at the end of the `test:*` list. Each step: write its
tests first and see them red for the right reason, then build, then green.

- **S2.0, the pin census (read only; no file changes).** For every file Stage

  anchors on the `line` prop lines), or the shape of a row. Report, per test:
  what it pins, whether the planned edit trips it, and the exact data
  amendment you propose. The founder rules on each one by one (rulings 64 and

- 18, 49: Stage 1 and Stage 2 go to main in ONE pull request and ONE deploy,

**Standing rules that carry over.** Ruling 64: an over-tight pin found at a
gate comes to the founder one by one, never fixed in a batch; red first, then
green; show the diff and say in plain words what the check still protects.
Ruling 71: the stop conditions end a run at once, namely any change to
InviteEvent or OutboundMessage from the baselines (21|0 on `gather_dev`, 22|0
on `gather_demo`, checked after every script), swap above 10.8 GB (wait,
resume only on his word), any change in the migrations table (59, 0
unfinished), a table count no security fixture explains, a connection to
anything but `gather_dev` or `gather_demo`, or an outbound attempt off the
guard's known list; any other red does not stop a run, so record it with its
first failing assertion and a cause (environment, over-tight pin or the
ticket's code) and keep going. Ruling 74: a tooling failure that fires none of
those may be fixed and resumed with the evidence kept, and is reported plainly
each time. The safe setup: Node 22 through the guard stand-in, provider keys
blanked per process, the egress guard and the OS wall (Chrome helper
restricted to localhost instead), the provider trap, nothing sent, nothing
near production, settings in the process environment only, never print a key
or a connection string. Zones 7 and 9 read only; nothing under
`src/app/api/auth/` changes; Zone 6 untouched; no new migration or API route
without a ruling. No commit, push or merge without the founder's approval in
the chat; commit with `git commit -F`, copies saved first and the hook
checked. Tools: `~/gather-gate-runbook/` and its `s17/` folder (see its
README).

to any other database is read together with rulings 95 and 97, on the

### The tests (named; none run in the plan)

Cover the child rule today:
- `test:child-exclusion` (`child-message-exclusion-test.ts`): every real
  recipient path against a child with a phone and an email, with an adult
  control. Extend with a decide-later subject; the control is the same child
  settled to "message".
- `test:child-tokens` (`child-token-exposure-test.ts`): links, directory,
  claim; layer N forbids the unsafe filter. Extend: no link, not in the
  directory, claim refused.
- `tests/child-assignment-eligibility-test.ts`: a kid with a job stays
  assignable (GTC-207). It has no npm script today; ruling 10 adds
  `test:child-assignment`.

Cover the messaging gates today (expected to stay green; extend where a
decide-later child changes nothing, to prove it): `test:channel-chooser`,
`test:ask-preview`, `test:carried-child-door`, `test:carried-answer`,
`test:carried-ask`, `test:press`, `test:preflight-withholding`,
`test:chase-channel`, `test:chase`, `test:board-not-chased`,
`test:decide-by-followup`, `test:decide-by-email`, `test:wrap-up` (and
`-dedupe`, `-quiet-hours`), `test:host-nudge`, `test:resend-delivery`,
`test:resend-errors`, `test:glance-read`, `test:glance-actions`,
`test:gone-quiet`, `test:hand-back`.

Cover household save and edit: `test:household-edit`,
`test:household-edit-integration`, `test:household-edit-toggle-fields` (the
model for the new field), `test:household-save` (the form and the browser
draft), `test:host-household`.

New checks:
1. Tripwire: no gate file names the marker (Stage 1).
2. Every existing row reads "no" (the rehearsal, not a unit test).
3. A decide-later child with a phone and an email is excluded on every path in
   `test:child-exclusion`; no link, not in the directory, claim refused;
   carried or host-listed exactly as a plain child; no ask row.
4. Round trip through all three doors: saved "yes", reload shows it, an edit of
   another field keeps it, ticking to message gives GUEST and "no", tick plus
   decide later gives the adult.
5. The confirm line appears only after the press and only for a decide-later
   child going to "message".
6. Pre-flight lists them, the step-3 fingerprint changes, `kidsNote` still
   lists them as kids, Send is unchanged by them.
7. A restored draft keeps the marker.
8. The settle blanking (ruling 17), at the edit door
   (`test:household-edit-toggle-fields` is the model): a household saved with
   a decide-later kid holding an email and a phone, then settled to "don't
   message", stores both as null, role CHILD, marker "no" (when the person has
   no opt-out on any event; with one, see item 12). The same on her own
   household, which settles through the same `PUT`: the host door is
   create-only (C11). `test:host-household` keeps covering that door's first
   save.
9. Settled to "message" instead: the email and phone are kept, role GUEST,
   marker "no". A decide-later kid saved again unchanged keeps both and stays
   "yes".
10. A plain child whose details were typed (marker "no") is untouched by a
    save: nothing blanks it (ruling 19). A child saved before this ticket and a
    kid typed with the tick off keep their details across a save. A new
    household (`POST`) never blanks anything.
11. The form (a browser-free render test in the style of
    `test:household-save`): turning Decide later off on a row saved as decide
    later, with the tick off, empties that row's email and phone boxes; the
    same on a row never saved as decide later does not, nor on a row with no
    `personEventId` (ruling 26); nothing is stored until Save; Cancel keeps the
    stored details; ticking instead keeps them.
12. The shared person and the opt-out outcome (rulings 27, 30, 33 and 34),
    pinned as ruled. The test creates two events with two hosts, both with a
    row on one person (email and phone), and settles the first host's
    decide-later child to "don't message" through the edit door. It creates
    all its rows and deletes only those. Cases:
    - (a) No opt-out anywhere: the person's email and phone are blanked, and
      the second host's row reads the person with neither (ruling 27).
    - (b) An `EmailOptOut` for the person on the FIRST host's event (this
      event): the phone is blanked, the **email is kept**, the opt-out row
      stays, and the second host's row still reads the email. Role CHILD,
      marker "no" (ruling 33). Nothing else on the person changes (name).
    - (c) An `EmailOptOut` only on the SECOND host's event, none on this one.
      **This case flipped with ruling 34:** the refusal applies, so the phone
      is blanked, the **email is kept**, the opt-out row stays, and the second
      host's row still reads the email. Before ruling 34 ("for this event") it
      blanked the email; this is the shared-person case that was the reason for
      the widening.
    - (d) The spam-report control: the address also held in `EmailBlock`. In
      (b) the email is kept and the block still holds the address; with the
      block alone (no opt-out) the settle blanks the email and the block still
      holds the address. The address is blocked either way, by address.
    - (e) An ordinary clear still clears: a plain row (marker no, staying no)
      with the email blanked and an opt-out on this event has its email
      nulled, as today; so does a decide-later row staying decide-later. This
      is the later item (A2) pinned as unchanged.
    - (f) A person with an `EmailOptOut` on TWO events (this one and the second
      host's): the email is kept, the phone is blanked, both opt-out rows
      stay, and nothing is written to either row. The same when the two
      opt-outs are on two other events and none on this one: the answer is
      "there is a row", so the count of rows does not matter.
13. W7 and the two-line row (ruling 28), in `test:preflight-fold`'s style: with
    step 3 folded the row shows `talkLine`'s line first and W7 under it, in the
    same amber, when both apply; W7 alone when only it applies; `talkLine`'s
    line alone when only it applies; neither draws a line element; the page
    still computes `talkLine` from the households with no channel. W8 shows on
    the saved card.
14. A plain child ticked to "message" after the press gets no confirm line; a
    decide-later child does (ruling 16).
15. No server guard (ruling 25): the edit door stores a decide-later child
    saved to "message" after the press whether or not any confirmation came
    with the request.
16. R4 as ruled (ruling 20): the control is not offered while the tick is on;
    a payload carrying both stores GUEST with the marker "no"; a payload that
    omits the marker clears it.
17. The two rehearsal checks of S1.4: the existing gate passes from a clean
    `128b341` clone against the migrated copy; the Stage 2 set passes against
    it too. Rehearsal runs, not unit tests.
18. The settle is recognised only as ruled (ruling 33), at the edit door, each
    with an opt-out on any event and a blank email arriving:
    - saved "yes", payload explicit false, tick off: a settle; the email is
      kept. The same with a non-blank email in the payload: written as sent.
    - saved "yes", payload omits the marker (a stale tab): the marker is
      cleared, it is not a settle, the email is nulled as today.
    - saved "yes", payload true: not a settle, the email is nulled as today.
    - saved "no", payload explicit false: not a settle, nulled as today.
    - saved "yes", payload explicit false, tick ON: not a settle; role GUEST;
      details as sent.
    - a row with no `personEventId`: not a settle. The page test: after a
      refused clear the saved card shows W9, once, and it is not shown after a
      settle that cleared the email, after an ordinary clear, or when the
      email comes back blank.
19. The refusal reads Zone 9's table from non-zone code and edits no Zone 9
    file. This holds with the any-event read. (i) A source scan:
    `updateExistingMember` in `reconcileMembers.ts` is the reader, by one

    `personId` alone (case 12(c) pins that it means any event);
    `reconcileMembers.ts` imports nothing from `email-opt-out.ts`,
    `email-block.ts` or any other Zone 9 file; nothing under the households
    code or the household routes calls `emailOptOut.create`, `createMany`,

20. STOP handling is untouched (ruling 50, B8). A diff check pinned to the
    base `128b341`, in the style of item 19(ii): the branch's diff adds or
    changes no line that names `SmsOptOut`, `smsOptedOut` or
    `opt-out-service`, and lists none of `opt-out-service.ts`, the TNZ webhook

    the settle clears a child's phone number and leaves any `SmsOptOut` row

**Stage 3 tests that rulings 51 and 52 fix.** Stage 3's tests are not written
here; they are the plan's (item 9 of "What the Stage 3 plan must settle").
These are named now so the plan carries them:
- A pick fills a new row and an unsaved row. Offered to a row that is already
  saved, it fills nothing: the row's name, email, phone and role are as they
  were, and the person behind the membership is unchanged (ruling 51).
- Her own row in host mode takes no pick: her name, phone and read-only email
  are as they were (ruling 51).
- Remove-and-add: removing a saved member and picking into a new row fills that
  new row only (ruling 51).
- The warning (ruling 52): it shows for a kid row with the tick off, not on
  decide later, whose details came from a pick this sitting. It does not show
  for a child saved before this ticket and holding details, for a kid typed
  with the tick off, for a row on decide later, or with the tick on. It informs
  and does not stop the save: the save goes ahead with the warning showing.
- No new API route (rulings 44 and 53): the diff adds no file under
  `src/app/api` and no entry to `route-classifications.json`; a new page under
  `src/app` is allowed. Item 20's diff check extends to the Stage 3 files in
  the same way.

**`test:sample-marker`, check M1 (ruling 64).** M1 asked that the GTC-426
migration sort straight after GTC-374's "and is the last". This ticket's
migration is later, so M1 went red for that reason alone. It is amended to
drop "and is the last" and keeps "sorts straight after". Red first, then green;
the other 18 checks are unchanged. `tests/sample-marker-test.ts` is in this
ticket's scope for that one amendment and nothing else.

**`test:tweaks-guard`, checks G17 and G18 (ruling 72).** GTC-414's guard holds
the protected zones byte for byte at `21a9577` plus a list of named, ruled
changes. G17 (the schema) and G18 (the migrations folder) went red at S1.7
because this ticket's Zone 5 change was not on that list. Ruling 72 adds it as
data only. `tests/tweaks-guard-test.ts` is in this ticket's scope for that one
amendment and nothing else.

**Test work that ruling 28 adds.** Widening `PreflightStep` from one line to
two touches tests that pin the old one-line shape. They are extended in this
ticket:
- `test:preflight-fold` (`preflight-fold-test.tsx`, GTC-377's own pin): the
  source check that the page computes step 3's line from
  `talkLine(households.filter(... channelFor(...)))`; the checks that a step
  with no line draws no `data-step-line` element; and the step 3 checks that
  expect `talkLine`'s text. New checks are added for W7 alone, W7 with
  `talkLine`'s line, and the order.
- `test:tweaks-d` (`tweaks-d-test.tsx`): it applies GTC-422's edits to a copy
  of `PreflightStep.tsx` as exact-text replacements, each of which must match
  exactly once. Two of the anchors are the `line` prop lines (`line = null,`
  and `line?: StepLine | null;`, each followed by `children`). If the new prop
  changes those lines, the test's anchors are updated with it; if the new prop
  is added after `children`, they stand. Either way the test is run and the
  edit applied.
- `test:demo-tweaks-6` (`demo-tweaks-6-test.tsx`): reads the first
  `header [data-step-line]` of step 2. Step 2 is untouched, so it should stay
  green; it is run, not assumed.
- `test:walkthrough-batch2` mentions GTC-377 around "step 3 starts closed": run
  and read, since step 3's row now carries a second line.

Expected to need a small edit, not to go red for a real reason: tests that
compare a whole helper row (`household-save-test.tsx`,
`demo-tweaks-2-test.tsx`, the client-payload test). The security suite (Zone
6) is not touched and should stay green, because children stay CHILD. Every

### The W-lines, as ruled

**W4 is replaced (ruling 89, 3 Nov 2026).** W4's words and the Save button's
label below are replaced by ruling 89. The text below is left as filed.

Every new sentence a host would read, drafted by the plan (ruling 7) and now
approved: W1 to W6 exactly as drafted (ruling 13); W7 and W8 as drafted, and
part of the ticket, not optional (ruling 14). W1's label was ruled earlier
(rulings 6 and 7). **W9 was drafted at ruling 33's request and is approved,
with "on this event" removed because of ruling 34 (ruling 35).** Each carries
the plan's flag for "decide by": the product already has a decide-by deadline
for a guest's "maybe"
(`decide-by.ts`; the board's DECIDE_BY_EXPIRED red; the maybe follow-up text).
A host could take "decide later" as that deadline.

The plan's general finding: the household form (Moment 1) and pre-flight step
3 sit before the press, where no guest has said "maybe", so a deadline reading
is unlikely there. The risk rises where "decide" appears beside a date, the
word "until", or a guest's reply, and on the board and the guest pages, which
this ticket does not touch. None of the sentences below uses "by", "deadline"
or a date.

- **W1 — the choice beside the tick on a kid row (ruling 6, ruling 7).**
  *"Decide later"*. The label is ruled. Flag: the bare label is the closest of
  all to "decide by"; it is accepted by ruling 7.
- **W2 — the line under the choice, shown when it is chosen.** *"Gather won't
  message {name} until you choose. You can change this any time."* Flag:
  "until you choose" is a condition, not a date, but "any time" is a promise;
  it should be true after the press too, and it is (the form still opens).
- **W3 — the note when the row is holding contact details.** *"Gather is
  keeping {name}'s contact details for now. It won't use them unless you
  choose to message {name}."* Flag: none. It is the plain statement of ruling
  2 and the plain-words section above, in the host's language. "Contact
  details" covers the number and the email: R1 is answered (ruling 15).
- **W4 — the confirm line, with its two buttons (ruling 5).** *"The
  invitations have already gone out. If you save this, Gather will message
  {name} shortly after."* Buttons: *"Save and message {name}"* and *"Go
  back"*. Flag: "already gone out" is about invitations, not a deadline.
  "Shortly after" is honest for the 2-minute cron; it is not a promise of a
  time.
- **W5 — the pre-flight step 3 line, one per household with decide-later
  kids.** *"Decide later: Josh, Mere"* beside the existing *"Kids: Josh,
  Mere"*. Flag: step 3 does not mention maybes, so the reading is unlikely.
- **W6 — the warning sentence that goes with W5 (ruling 3's "warn").**
  *"Gather won't message them unless you change this in their household."*
  Flag: none. It names what happens, with no date. Ruling 4 says read-only, so
  there is no link; this sentence says where to go.
- **W7 — the closed-row line for a folded step 3 (approved, ruling 14).** *"1
  kid is still on decide later."* / *"{n} kids are still on decide later."*
  Modelled on `looseLine` for step 1. A folded step can be ticked unopened, so
  without it a folded step 3 shows nothing. Step 3's row already carries one
  line (`talkLine`) and has one slot; both show, `talkLine`'s first and W7
  under it in the same amber (ruling 28). Flag: as W5.
- **W8 — the household card, Moment 1 (approved, ruling 14).**
  *"Josh (decide later)"* beside the kid's name on the saved household card.
  Flag: as W1.

- **W9 — the line after a refused clear (approved, ruling 35).** Exactly:
  *"We kept {name}'s email address on file, because there is a no-email note
  for them."* The draft had ended "on this event"; ruling 35 removed those
  words because the opt-out may be on any event (ruling 34).
  - *Where and when (ruled).* A quiet line on that household's saved card, no
    button, shown only after a refused clear: when the server kept an email
    that the form had blanked while settling a decide-later child to "don't
    message" (S2.3). It stays until she next opens the household or leaves the
    page. No second line for the phone.
  - *Why this wording.* It says what happened (kept), gives the reason in plain
    words, and says nothing about sending. It avoids "unsubscribed", "opted
    out", "stop", "again", "sent" and "emailed", each of which would suggest a
    message had gone to a child. "A no-email note" is a record on file, not an
    event. It does not say the child will be messaged either: the row is a
    don't-message child.
  - *Other wordings put to the founder, not chosen.* (2) *"We kept {name}'s
    email address on file, as
    they have asked not to get email about this event."* "Asked not to get"
    suggests they were emailed. (3) *"We kept {name}'s email address on file.
    Everything else you cleared is cleared."* Safe, but gives her no reason,
    and she may think the settle failed.
  - *Flag for "decide by".* None. It uses no "decide" and no date.
  - *A second line for the phone?* No. The sentence reports the one thing that
    differs from what she did. The phone was cleared as she asked, and the
    card and the reopened household show it blank; a line saying so would only
    repeat her own action. The email is the surprise.

The sentence already under the tick, *"Kids never get messages from Gather.
Tick this only if {name} should hear from us directly rather than through an
adult."*, stays as it is. It is still true of a decide-later child. It is not a
new sentence.

Stage 3's sentences are not drafted. They are the founder's item 11 above.

### Claude Code's corrections to the ticket

Found while planning, 2026-11-02, and recorded here. The brief is left as
filed. C1 to C7 are the plan's corrections; the founder asked for them to be
noted (C4 and C5 in particular). C8 was found while this ticket was written;
C9 and C10 while the third set of rulings was applied; C11 while ruling 33 was
applied; C12 to C14 while rulings 36 to 50 were applied.

1. **C1 — the session was not on main.** It was on `docs/gtc-448-close`
   (`625766e`). `origin/main` (`128b341`) is a merge commit with an identical
   file tree (empty diff), so what the plan read equals main. The local `main`
   was stale (`df782c4`) and the local `master` stale (`ebc21d9`); the plan's
   fetch also pruned `origin/master`, which no longer exists upstream.
   **Fixed:** this work is on `feat/contact-import-screen`, cut from
   `origin/main` `128b341`.
2. **C2 — the constants name `master`.** `GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md` still
   names `master` (Deploy) and `feat/moment-one-redesign` (Base Branch). The
   trunk is `main`. `ci.yml`'s push trigger names `master` (pull requests
   still run). Which branch Railway builds cannot be confirmed from the repo;
   it is item 1 of the Railway checklist, which the founder confirms (ruling
   48).
3. **C3 — "Old enough to message directly" is not a stored field.** It exists
   only in the form and the save request (`adultRoled`). The saved facts are
   the role and `isYoungPerson`.
4. **C4 — no existing child will show as decide later after the change.**
   Every row starts at "no". A kid she never considered is stored the same as
   one she ruled on: the tick off is also the default. That is correct, and
   the founder may expect otherwise. Only a child saved through Stage 2's
   choice can be "yes".
5. **C5 — the contact-reading code is in `src/lib/contact-import/`, not
   `src/lib/contact/`.** Nothing imports it yet, and the picking screen and
   the prompt do not exist. GTC-448 says roles are set only in the household
   form, so **GTC-448's pick prompt must write into the form's kid-row state,
   not set a role itself.**
6. **C6 — the nudges cron sends nothing.** It queues rows. Real sends come from
   `outbound-dispatch`, `wrap-up-dispatch` and `decide-by-followups`.
7. **C7 — `tests/child-assignment-eligibility-test.ts` has no npm script**, so
   the "every `test:*` script" gate does not run it. Ruling 10 adds
   `test:child-assignment`.
8. **C8 — the run sheet is not in the repo.** Searched 2026-11-02. The
   run-sheet steps and decisions named in the rulings (A5, A7, A8, A11, A13,
   B8, C1, C2, C7, D8 to D14) are known only from the founder's message. Stage
   3 rests on it.
9. **C9 — a step's row line shows folded or open.** Ruling 22 puts W7 "on its
   folded row". `PreflightStep` draws the row line in the header for a folded
   step and an open one alike, so W7 (like `talkLine`'s line today) shows on
   step 3's row whether the step is folded or open. In the open step it sits
   above W5 and W6 in the body, which say the same thing at more length. Not a
   fault; recorded so it is not a surprise.
10. **C10 — two tests pin the old one-line row by its source text.**
    `test:tweaks-d` applies GTC-422's edits to `PreflightStep.tsx` as
    exact-text replacements anchored on the `line` prop lines, and
    `test:preflight-fold` checks by pattern how the page calls `talkLine`.
    Ruling 28 widens the row to two lines, so both are extended with it (see
    "The tests").
11. **C11 — her own household's host door is create-only, so it cannot
    settle.** Earlier text in this ticket said her own household's settle goes
    through `host-household/route.ts` and `hostHousehold.ts` "into the same
    function". That is true of its first save, but `createHostHousehold` says
    it is "a create path for new events only" and hands
    `reconcileHouseholdMembers` only her own membership as existing, so kid
    rows there go through `createNewMember`, never `updateExistingMember`. A
    settle on her household goes through `PUT households/[householdId]`, like
    every other household. S2.3 and test item 8 are corrected; S2.1 still
    counts the host door as a door that writes the marker on a first save.
12. **C12 — ruling 47 against the constants' Deploy caveat.**
    `GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md` (Deploy) says to rehearse any migration
    "against a copy of the production database before triggering a deploy that
    carries one". Ruling 47 takes the copy from a fresh dump of the founder's
    local `gather_dev` and never from production, which is what GTC-189 slice
    4, GTC-289 and GTC-448 did, and what this session's own rule (never
    connect to production) requires of the plan. The two differ in kind:
    `gather_dev` is a smaller, different data set, and a local Postgres may
    not be the production version. The ticket follows ruling 47 and adds
    checklist item 3 (read the production Postgres version) to cover the part
    a local copy cannot show. The constants file is not edited (it needs the
    founder's approval in chat).
13. **C13 — "no new route" and "a page of its own".** Ruling 44 says Stage 3
    needs no new route; A13 and C1 give the contacts screen a page of its own.
    A page is a new route in Next's sense. Read here as no new API route,
    which is what the earlier rulings meant. Checked: middleware and the
    security route scan do not cover a page elsewhere (see "What the code
    already gives Stage 3"). **Confirmed by ruling 53:** ruling 44 means no new
    API route, and a new page is fine.
14. **C14 — chore 16 against the constants' `.env.local` rule.**
    `GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md` (the live layer of `test:security`) says to
    start the dev server with the provider keys and the AI key blanked inline
    and "never edit `.env.local`" (founder ruling Q7, 2026-10-06). The
    founder's chore 16 moves the real provider keys from `.env` to
    `.env.local`, which is that edit. The move answers GTC-448's open item
    (real secrets in `.env`, which Prisma reads and so refills blanked
    variables), so it is wanted, but it needs the constants' line brought up
    to date with the founder's approval, and it is the founder's to do or to
    hand over explicitly. Nothing in this ticket does it, and no key is
    printed. **Confirmed by ruling 54:** chore 16 includes bringing the
    constants line about `.env.local` up to date. That is its own small docs
    change, with the founder's approval at that time, and not part of this
    ticket.

### S2.0 census findings, 3 Nov 2026 (findings, not rulings)

Made at `dc92223`, read only. Five read-only helpers read the tests in slices,
and the main claims were checked against the test source. No file was changed
and no test was run, so "green today" is assumed, apart from GTC-448's nine
known reds. The browser layers of some tests were judged from their fixtures
and source, not run. These are findings, not rulings: rulings 82 to 84 answer
the pins and ruling 85 holds the W4 question. Where a finding corrects an
earlier passage of this ticket, that passage is left as filed.

1. **F1 — `tweaks-d` P1 and P2 are byte pins.** In `tests/tweaks-d-test.tsx`,
   P1 (line 561) and P2 (line 566) require the live
   `src/app/plan/[eventId]/pre-flight/page.tsx` and
   `src/components/preflight/PreflightStep.tsx` each to equal commit `3b8cdfa`
   plus the typed edit lists `PAGE_EDITS` (line 141) and `STEP_EDITS` (line
   173), byte for byte (`exactly`, line 443). Any Stage 2 edit to either file
   trips them, so S2.5 trips both. The anchors are applied to the fixed old
   copy, which never moves, so the `tweaks-d` entry under "The tests" is wrong
   about the cause: what trips is the equality of the live file, not the
   anchors. P3 also counts the words `useState` and `useEffect` over the whole
   page text, comments included, so S2.5 adds no hook and no comment that uses
   either word. Ruling 82 approves the amendment as data only.
2. **F2 — `tweaks-f` P1 is the same kind of pin, and was not in the plan.**
   `tests/tweaks-f-test.tsx` P1 (line 313) requires the live pre-flight page to
   equal `withD(base.replace(AFTER_WAITING, () => AFTER_WAITING + BUTTON))`,
   where the base is `8bc1064`, `withD` (line 136) applies `GTC422_EDITS` (line
   113), and BUTTON is the typed Ticket F button. Any S2.5 edit to the page
   trips it. P3 (`beforeSendOnly`, line 342) counts `sentAt`, `useState` and
   `useEffect` in the page, comments included, so S2.5 adds none of them.
   Ruling 82 approves the amendment as data only.
3. **F3 — the list of tests under ruling 28.** Ruling 28's own words are "the
   tests that pin GTC-377's row lines"; it names none. The four names in S2.0
   and S2.5 (`preflight-fold`, `tweaks-d`, `demo-tweaks-6`,
   `walkthrough-batch2`) are the plan's reading. The census finds: only
   `preflight-fold` pins step 3's row line (its S5, R6 and C7); `tweaks-d`
   pins no row line in meaning, only the byte pins of F1; `demo-tweaks-6` pins
   step 2's line (the first `header [data-step-line]`), and step 3 is not
   touched by it; `walkthrough-batch2` pins the shape of step 3's household
   rows and has no row-line check. `tweaks-f` (F2) is a fifth test the list
   missed. The "small edit" the plan expected for `household-save`,
   `demo-tweaks-2` and the client-payload test is not needed: they need
   placement rules in the build, not changes to the tests.
4. **F4 — Pin 5, `preflight-fold`.** No existing check needs amending if
   `line` stays a single `{ text, amber }` object and the second line arrives
   through a new optional prop. Then one line draws byte-identical markup, no
   line draws no `data-step-line`, the `talkLine(households.filter(...
   channelFor(` text stays literal in the page, and both lines sit inside the
   toggle. The new checks for W7 alone, W7 with `talkLine`'s line, and the
   order are additions. The suite already has one known red, C9.
5. **F5 — Pin 6, checks red only on an uncommitted tree.** These compare the
   working folder with `HEAD`, so they go red while a listed path has
   uncommitted edits and are green once the step is committed. No amendment.
   They are run after each step's commit, not before: `demo-tweaks` B5 (`git
   diff --quiet HEAD -- src/app/api`, the whole API folder), B7 (the same for
   `Moment1Summary.tsx`, only if that file is touched), `demo-sandbox-b` S11
   (the whole `src/lib/preflight` folder, which holds `tick-memory.ts`) and
   `walkthrough-batch11` S2 (the pre-flight route and `workflow.ts`).
6. **F6 — the tick fingerprint.** `preflightPrints` in
   `src/lib/preflight/tick-memory.ts` builds step 3's print from each member's
   id, name and nudge mark. If the marker were added for every member, every
   stored step-3 tick on every event would un-tick once after the deploy. So
   the marker is added to a member's entry only when it is yes. No test pins an
   exact print: `walkthrough-batch16` P7 to P9 use samples without the marker
   and check only the format (8 hex characters) and that the print moves.
7. **F7 — the "invitations have gone" signal.** The setup page already reads
   `Event.sentAt` (the database column `inviteSendConfirmedAt`; the schema
   calls it THE SEND) in several places (lines 437, 649, 1032, 1501, 1722 and
   1737). S2.4's confirm line (W4) can use it, with no new field. Whether W4's
   words are true is a separate question, held by ruling 85.
8. **F8 — where the outbound cron runs.** The outbound cron's schedule, every
   two minutes, is in `vercel.json`. This session could not tell from the
   source whether Railway runs it every two minutes. Ruling 48's Railway
   checklist is where to confirm it.
9. **F9 — the W4 trace, and a correction to ruling 16's note.** From the
   W4 investigation (ruling 85), read from the source; nothing was run.
   - **The correction.** "Later items" says a plain child ticked to "message"
     after the press has "the same effect today: `enrolMiniSends` queues an
     ask for them within minutes" (ruling 16). That is true only once the
     child holds a link. With no link the sweep counts the child as awaiting
     a link and skips them. The note is left as filed.
   - **What the save does.** The household save changes a ticked child's
     stored role from CHILD to GUEST, in place (`reconcileMembers.ts`). It
     makes no link and writes no message row. The three household routes,
     `reconcileMembers.ts` and `hostHousehold.ts` were searched by text for a
     link, message or enrolment call, and hold none.
   - **Where a link comes from.** Only `ensureEventTokens` (`tokens.ts`)
     creates access tokens. It has four callers: the press; the move to
     CONFIRMING, which is before the press; `GET /api/events/[id]/tokens`;
     and the person-edit route, only when it changes a role or a team. The
     tokens route is called only by the old plan page, which the board
     reaches through its "Invites & people" door.
   - **Case A, settled before the press.** The press makes the link first,
     inside its own transaction, and then reads the recipients. A GUEST with
     a usable email or NZ mobile is a direct recipient and is messaged like
     any adult. W4 is not shown.
   - **Case B, settled after the press.** The save makes no link. Every two
     minutes the sweep (`enrolMiniSends`) finds the child with no link,
     counts them as awaiting a link and skips them. Its own note refuses to
     make links from the cron, and `mini-send-test` layer L pins that. When
     the host next opens "Invites & people" the link is made. The next run
     then enrols the child, and the same run sends.
   - **Parity.** An adult added to a household after the press is in the
     same state today, through the same household form.
   - **Limits.** Only events pressed by the new press, which have invitation
     rows, are swept; sample events never are. The child needs a usable email
     or NZ mobile that is not opted out or blocked, and the send checks again
     at send time (a missing reply-to address, a block). Email goes first,
     then text, and a text waits through quiet hours, 9pm to 8am NZ, until
     8:05am. The cron schedule is in `vercel.json`, and whether Railway runs
     it is F8. The household routes and `hostHousehold.ts` were searched, not
     read in full.
   - **Open, not built and not ruled.** First, a later-ticket idea: make the
     link on save once the event has been pressed (option 4 of the W4
     report). Second, the comment in the sweep (`dispatch.ts`) that makes
     minting wait on GTC-316 may be stale, because `tokens.ts` already carries
     GTC-316's child rule. Whether GTC-316 is closed was not checked. Neither
     is part of Stage 2.
   - **Files read, in part or in full:** `src/lib/tokens.ts`,
     `src/lib/eligibility/child-exclusion.ts`,
     `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`,
     `src/lib/households/reconcileMembers.ts`, `src/lib/press/dispatch.ts`,
     `src/lib/press/press.ts` (lines 240 to 350),
     `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts`,
     `src/app/api/cron/outbound-dispatch/route.ts`,
     `src/app/api/events/[id]/tokens/route.ts`,
     `src/app/plan/[eventId]/page.tsx` (lines 355 to 372 and 555 to 610),
     `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/route.ts` (lines 225 to 305),
     `src/app/api/events/[id]/pre-flight/message/route.ts` (the header note),
     `src/lib/workflow.ts` (lines 760 to 800), `src/lib/events/home-href.ts`,
     `src/lib/sms/quiet-hours.ts` (the hours) and `vercel.json` (the cron
     lines).
   - **Files searched by text only:** the three household routes,
     `src/lib/households/hostHousehold.ts`,
     `src/app/api/events/[id]/invite-status/route.ts`,
     `src/components/glance/GlanceBoard.tsx`, `tests/mini-send-test.ts`
     (layer L), and all of `src/` for callers of `ensureEventTokens`, the
     sweep and token creation.

### S2.0 build rules, 3 Nov 2026 (findings, not rulings)

Ruling 87 asks for these. They are what the census found the Stage 2 build
must obey so that no existing check goes red for a wrong reason. They were
read from the test source at `1b4b9b5`, and the counts were checked against
the source on 3 Nov 2026; nothing was run. "Stays N" means a check counts it.
"Comments included" means a check reads the raw file. These are findings, not
rulings. A rule that blocks something the founder wants is brought to the
founder one by one (ruling 64); the check is not edited to fit.

**The form (`Moment1InputForm.tsx`, `form-draft.ts`).**
- `household-save` S9: the bodies of `handleAddAnother` and `handleEditSave`
  must equal commit `9ff914d`, comments stripped. Only `buildPayload` may
  change. So the W4 confirm goes on the Save buttons' `onClick`, or on a
  wrapper, and never inside those two bodies.
- `household-save` S1 and S2: `if (checksFail()) return;` stays at 4 and
  `hasValidationErrors()` at 1. "Go back" must not call `resetForm();`, which
  stays at 3.
- `aria-invalid={` stays at 9 and `scrollIntoView` at 1. There is no `fetch(`
  and no `useRouter` in the form or in `form-draft.ts`. The new control has no
  `aria-invalid` and no `role="alert"`, and the name, email and phone boxes
  stay inside the existing `border-l-4` row.
- `household-save` S10: only `Moment1InputForm.tsx` and `Providers.tsx` may
  mention `households/form-draft`. So `decideLater` goes on the exported
  `Moment1PersonInput`, and no other source file imports from `form-draft`.
- `demo-tweaks-3` B4: `moveOnRef.current = handleDone` and `handleDone` keep
  their shape, and `onClick={handleDone}` is not written in the form.
- `walkthrough-batch12` S6: no fixed overlay for the confirm line; it sits in
  the page flow.

**The setup page (`setup/page.tsx`).**
- `household-save` S12: the `resumeEdit={{ savedIds: ..., open: handleEdit, }}`
  object and `data.households.map(apiHouseholdToSaved)` keep their shape.
  `apiHouseholdToSaved` stays a one-argument function passed straight to

### Open items

and the Stage 3 design points are ruled (rulings 36 to 45, with rulings 51 and

must settle (see "What the Stage 3 plan must settle").

  the first. Any other test that pins "the last migration", or the exact text
  of `PersonEvent`, and goes red because of this ticket comes back to the

  and G18 (the migrations folder), which hold Zone 5 at `21a9577` plus

- **The Railway checklist (ruling 48).** The founder confirms which branch

- **A5**, the wording for how Gather treats contacts. Not ruled. Owner: the

- **The Stage 3 plan**, with its W-lines (ruling 45), its tests, and its

**Settled by the seventh message and taken off this list:** the merge style
(ruling 49: a merge commit); the green check before S0 (ruling 46); the source
of the rehearsal copy (ruling 47); "which branch Railway builds" (now item 1
of the checklist, ruling 48).

**Settled by the eighth message and marked ruled in the Stage 3 list:** a pick
into a saved row and into her own main contact (ruling 51); the trigger and the
