the reading of "no new route" (ruling 53). Still open: what "placed" means
(clash 7, item 2 of the list).

**Chores.** The founder's items 15 to 19, recorded as open items and not as
rulings for this ticket, verbatim:

> 15. Severity of GTC-448 and GTC-449 is mine to set.
> 16. Move the real provider keys from .env to .env.local before
>     GTC-449's full test run. Do it without printing any key.
> 17. CI trigger: point the push trigger at main. A separate chore after
>     GTC-449.
> 18. The six dependency vulnerabilities GitHub reports: a separate chore
>     after GTC-449.
> 19. Tidy-up of scratch folders, the clean clone and the backup dump
>     waits until GTC-449 ships. The backup dump stays until then.

The plan's notes on them:
- **15, severity.** Settled by rulings 55 and 56: GTC-449 is high and GTC-448
  is low, and `severity:` is set to match in the frontmatter of both tickets.
  GTC-448's body still says "Severity: not set" in two places; the founder
  asked for the frontmatter only, so those two lines are left as written.
- **16, the keys.** Before this ticket's full `test:*` run. It is the edit
  that the constants say never to make (C14), so the constants' line needs
  bringing up to date with the founder's approval. It answers GTC-448's open
  item (real secrets in `.env`). No key is printed, here or in any evidence.
  Ruling 54 confirms the constants' line is part of the chore, as its own
  small docs change with the founder's approval at that time, not part of this
  ticket. Ruling 70: chore 16 is NOT done before S1.7, which runs with the
  keys where they are so that it is comparable to GTC-448's record; it is done
  before S2.9, the Stage 2 gate. `.env` is not touched and `.env.local` is not
  created before then.
- **17, the CI trigger.** `ci.yml`'s push trigger names `master` (C2); pull
  requests still run. A separate chore after this ticket.
- **18, the six vulnerabilities.** A separate chore after this ticket. GTC-448
  recorded `npm audit` reporting 21 on 1 Nov 2026 (17 moderate, 4 high), and
  GitHub reports six; the counts come from different tools and are not
  reconciled here.
- **19, tidy-up.** Waits until this ticket ships, and the backup dump stays
  until then. That includes what this ticket's own steps make: the rehearsal
  dump and throwaway database (S1.3) and the clean clone (S1.4a).

**Later items** (recorded, not in this ticket's stages):

- **A plain child ticked to "message" after the press gets no confirm line**
  (ruling 16). It has the same effect today: `enrolMiniSends` queues an ask for
  them within minutes. Recorded as the gap the founder asked to be kept.
- **`AfterThePress` does not list decide-later children** (ruling 22). The
  household form and the card marker are where she finds them after the press.
- **Children saved before this ticket, and kids typed with the tick off, keep
  any contact details** they hold (ruling 19). Ruling 17's "only a decide-later
  child holds contact details" is true of the flows this ticket builds and not
  of those rows (confirmed, ruling 31). No backfill.
- **The blanking can reach another host's guest list through a shared person**
  (ruling 27, accepted). It is what the edit door already does for every edit;
  a test pins it. Its one sharper consequence, an unsubscribed address that can
  be emailed again, is Named risk 1 (ruling 30), closed for the settle path by
  rulings 33 and 34, on any event. A change to how a person is matched across
  events (GTC-293's ruled trade) is not this ticket's.
- **Today's ordinary clear can still strand an unsubscribe (ruling 33).** A
  host who clears the email of a person who unsubscribed from an event, on a
  plain row or on a decide-later row that stays decide-later, nulls it as
  before, and an address typed again on a new row then becomes a new person
  without the unsubscribe. Ruling 33 leaves this exactly as it is and records
  it as a separate later item, **to close with option A2 in a later ticket**:
  refuse the email blank on every edit when an `EmailOptOut` exists (on any
  event, as ruling 34 now reads it for the settle). A2
  changes what an ordinary edit does (a host could no longer clear an
  unsubscribed guest's email), so it is not part of this ticket.
- **A second save undoes the protection (ruling 33).** After a refused settle
  the kid is a plain row holding the kept email; clearing that email box and
  saving is today's ordinary clear and is not refused. Part of the item above.
- **The two stored-tier counts ignore role** (ruling 100; a candidate for a
  later ticket, not built and not ruled as a ticket). A settle that keeps an
  email stores the kid as EMAIL and DIRECT, and two readers count that tier
  without looking at role: the reachability breakdown and the trackable
  assignments in `invite-status/route.ts`, and the "Only N% of guests have
  confirmed" warning in `workflow.ts`. Nothing sends, because the role gate
  stops a CHILD on every message path. The candidate is to make the two
  counts role-aware. The same is true today of any kid whose email or phone
  was typed in with the tick off.
- **Straight apostrophes in W2, W3 and W9, curly elsewhere in the form**
  (ruling 103; W9 added at S2.3's form half). The three ruled sentences are
  drawn with straight apostrophes, exactly as the ticket has them, while other
  sentences in the same form use `&rsquo;` and the draft words use curly ones.
  A later polish that would change them together: checks W.1, W.2, 18.20 and
  18.22 and mutations S10 and C2 pin the straight form and would need a
  ruling. Not built, and not ruled as a ticket.

## Evidence (Executor-Completed)

**Not built.** Nothing is committed, pushed or deployed. Filled in per stage as
each lands.

### The start of the branch — the script

Done, 3 Nov 2026 (rulings 32 and 46; the founder approved the commit).

- **Result: green.** `tests/child-assignment-eligibility-test.ts` run once, by
  path, on this branch. Its tree equals `128b341` apart from this ticket file
  (`git diff 128b341 HEAD` listed only `docs/tickets/GTC-449.md`), so it counts
  as a run on main. 17 passed, 0 failed, exit 0. Then `npm run
  test:child-assignment`: 17 passed, 0 failed, exit 0.
- **What it asserts:** 5 database checks (a CHILD-role "kid with a job" has a
  `PersonEvent`, passes the same-team check, can be assigned an item, keeps
  the assignment after a round trip, and really is CHILD); 9 source reads (the
  host, coordinator-token and auto-assign routes import neither the
  child-exclusion nor the host-exclusion module, the same-team module does not
  import the host-exclusion module, and the host and token routes carry no
  `householdRole` gate); and 3 calls of the pure function `mayHoldRow` (the
  host may hold on any team, a child is still assignable, a guest on the wrong
  team is still refused).
- **The setup:** Node 22.23.4 through the guard stand-in; the outbound-traffic
  guard loaded in 3 processes per run with 0 refusals; inside the OS-level
  wall (`sandbox-exec`, every non-loopback IP refused); provider keys blanked
  in the process, a made-up Stripe key and webhook secret, `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS`
  set nowhere; local `gather_dev` only (checked: local, database `gather_dev`,
  58 of 58 migrations applied, none unfinished). The walls were proved first
  with made-up hosts and the reserved test address only: the OS wall alone
  refused with `EPERM`, the guard refused with `EGRESS_REFUSED`, and loopback
  worked. No key or connection string was printed.
- **Database counts, before, after the by-path run and after the npm run:**
  identical in all 12 tables read: User 4, Event 11, Person 196, PersonEvent
  170, Household 35, Team 34, Item 199, Assignment 132, InviteEvent 21,
  OutboundMessage 0, SmsOptOut 0, EmailOptOut 0. No `GTC207` rows were left
  behind, and the list of databases was unchanged (`gather_demo`, `gather_dev`,
  `kiwi_torch_dev`, `kiwi_torch_test`, `postgres`).
- **The change:** one line in `package.json`,
  `"test:child-assignment": "tsx tests/child-assignment-eligibility-test.ts"`,
  added at the end of the `test:*` list (166 scripts); the line before it gains
  its comma. Nothing else in the file changed, and the hook's prettier step
  changed nothing.
- **Commit:** `ebb16df`, `test(GTC-449): add the test:child-assignment script
  (S0, rulings 24, 32 and 46)`. Local only, not pushed.

### Stage 1 — the marker and its migration

In progress, 3 Nov 2026. Nothing is applied to `gather_dev`.

- **S1.1 and S1.2, done.** `messagingUndecided` is added to `model
  PersonEvent` after `isYoungPerson`, with the doc comment the ticket asked
  for. The migration was generated with `prisma migrate dev --create-only
  --skip-generate`, in the guarded setup. It needed a shadow database, which
  Prisma made and dropped itself (the database list is the same five as
  before); nothing was applied and `prisma generate` was not run. `prisma
  validate` and `prisma format --check` are clean. The SQL, approved as
  generated (ruling 57), is identical to an offline preview made from the old
  and new schema files with `prisma migrate diff` and no database:

```sql -- AlterTable ALTER TABLE "PersonEvent" ADD COLUMN
"messagingUndecided" BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false; ```

- **Committed** as `298c9c1` (ruling 59), two files, 12 insertions:
  `prisma/schema.prisma` and
  `prisma/migrations/20261102205933_gtc449_person_event_messaging_undecided/`
  (the folder's date is UTC). The hook found nothing to run on them, and the
  committed files equal the copies saved before.

- **S1.3, the rehearsal: green** (3 Nov 2026; rulings 47 and 58), in the
  guarded setup of S0.
  - *The dump.* `~/gather-db-backups/gather_dev-20261103-1011.dump`, 183,296
    bytes, 336 entries, taken with `pg_dump --format=custom --no-owner
    --no-acl` as the 1 Nov backup was. It stays (chore 19). *The copy.*
    `gather_dev_rehearsal_gtc449`, not one of the founder's five databases.
    The restore ran with no error and no message; all 39 tables had the same
    row counts as `gather_dev`, and so did the migrations table (58 applied,
    none unfinished) and the content of every `PersonEvent` column (a hash).
  - *The migration.* Before `prisma migrate deploy` the connection check
    printed "points at the copy: yes | local: yes | db:
    gather_dev_rehearsal_gtc449". `migrate deploy` applied the one migration
    to the copy only; `migrate status` then said "Database schema is up to
    date", and a drift check of the copy against `schema.prisma` said "No
    difference detected". The guard loaded in 4 processes and refused nothing.
  - *Metadata only.* The `PersonEvent` file id (`pg_class.relfilenode`) was
    620795 before and 620795 after. The transaction id and physical position
    of all 170 rows are identical before and after (a hash of both), so no row
    was rewritten or updated; the catalog marks the new column as a stored
    fast default. The content of every old column is identical. Every other
    data table has the same count; only the migrations table grew, 58 to 59.
    The whole structure, dumped before and after, differs by one line, the new
    column.
  - *The column.* From the catalog: `boolean`, not null, default `false`,
    exactly the SQL. `PersonEvent` went from 25 to 26 columns. Every existing
    row reads false: 170 rows, 0 true, 170 false, 0 empty.
  - *Probes, in one transaction rolled back.* An insert written the way the
    old code writes it, never naming the column, works and reads false.
    Setting true works and reads back, on a new row and on an existing one.
    Setting it empty is refused, in an update and in an insert, with the
    not-null error. A read that names only the old columns works. After the
    rollback: 170 rows, 0 true, 0 probe rows, and every fingerprint identical
    to just after the migration.
  - *Cleaned up.* The copy was dropped and is gone; the database list is the
    original five. `gather_dev` passed the same checks as before and stricter
    ones: migrations table 58 applied, 0 unfinished, same latest; the row
    counts of all 39 tables identical; the new column absent; the
    `PersonEvent` file id unchanged at 289266; the row fingerprints identical;
    the whole structure identical (2916 lines, the random `\restrict` lines of
    PostgreSQL 15.15's `pg_dump` removed). No shadow database and no stray
    Prisma process remained.
  - *One limit.* "Metadata only" is proved on PostgreSQL 15.15, the local
    version. It needs version 11 or later in production, which is item 3 of
    the Railway checklist.

- **M1 in `test:sample-marker`, red then green** (3 Nov 2026; ruling 64).
  - *Found by reading, before any run.* M1 asked that the GTC-426 migration
    "sorts straight after" GTC-374's "and is the last". The new migration
    folder is later, so M1 cannot hold. The helper `sortsRightAfter` ended
    with `i + 2 === sorted.length`. The test is a `gather_demo` suite (it
    refuses any other database), and it reads no `PersonEvent` column, so the
    client change does not touch it.
  - *Red.* The unamended test, run once against `gather_demo` (rolled-back
    probes only): 18 passed, 1 failed, exit 1. M1 was the only red; P1, S1 to
    S4, M2, M3, N1, D1 to D6, C1 to C3 and R1 were green.
  - *The amendment.* In `tests/sample-marker-test.ts`, `sortsRightAfter` loses
    `&& i + 2 === sorted.length` and gains a comment saying a later migration
    may follow (ruling 64), and M1's label loses ", and is the last". Nothing
    else in the file changed; the file is Prettier-clean. M1 still protects
    one thing: the GTC-426 migration sorts straight after GTC-374's in the
    folder order, so nobody has put a migration between them or moved it. A
    later migration, such as this ticket's, is allowed.
  - *Green.* The amended test, run once: 19 passed, 0 failed, exit 0. A diff
    of the two runs' check lists shows the M1 line and nothing else.
    `gather_demo` after both runs is identical to right after the apply
    (counts of all 39 tables, file id, row identities, structure;
    InviteEvent|OutboundMessage 22|0), and no probe row was left. The guard
    loaded in 3 and 4 processes and refused nothing.

- **S1.6, the tripwire test: written and green** (3 Nov 2026; rulings 67 and
  69), committed as `040ec47` (commit 1 of ruling 69):
  `tests/marker-tripwire-test.ts` and one `package.json` line,
  `"test:marker-tripwire": "tsx tests/marker-tripwire-test.ts"`, appended as
  the last of the `test:*` list. It needs no database, server or network and
  names no database.
  - *What it guards.* 90 files that send, make links, mint tokens or count
    people: every file in six folders (`src/lib/eligibility`, `press`, `sms`,
    `glance`, `email-delivery` and `src/app/api/cron`, 65 files) and 25 listed
    one by one (`wrap-up.ts`, `tokens.ts`, `plan-input.ts`, the ask preview
    and its composer, the chase choice, the test email, the send, nudge,
    resend, wrap-up and message routes, the directory and claim routes,
    `contact-fix.ts`, `households/channel.ts`, `moment3/people.ts`,
    `ask-register.ts`, `chase-hand-back.ts`, `send-health.ts` and the board
    strip). Anything added later to those folders is scanned too. It looks for
    the name `messagingUndecided` in any case, with or without an underscore,
    in code or in a comment.
  - *The reason, in the file's header.* The marker is the host's own note. It
    is not a rule: `householdRole` stays the sole gate. Stage 2's save routes,
    form, card and pre-flight page may name it, because they only write it,
    read it back and show it. The gate files decide who is messaged, linked or
    counted, so they must never be able to see it. A check (P4) refuses a file
    on both lists.
  - *The checks.* P1 to P5: all 90 listed files exist (a missing file fails,
    so a rename cannot shrink the list), all six folders hold files, no file
    is listed twice, no Stage 2 screen is also a gate file, and the scan
    covers 90 files. G1 and G2: none names the marker, and every file was
    read. K1 to K7, the positive controls: the scan finds the marker in the
    schema, inside `model PersonEvent` as a Boolean with a default, and in the
    migration SQL; it sees the other spellings a careless edit might use; it
    stays quiet on ordinary child-rule text; and the list check and the
    overlap check can both go red. B, the break proofs: for every one of the
    90 files, an in-memory copy with the marker put at the top, in the middle
    and at the end goes red. No real file is touched.
  - *Green:* 104 passed, 0 failed, exit 0, on the real tree, run in the
    guarded setup (the guard loaded in 4 processes and refused nothing).
  - *Red, on scratch copies of `src/` and `prisma/` outside the repo*, as the
    ticket's S1.6 asks. One deliberate line `pe.messagingUndecided` added to
    `src/lib/sms/send-sms.ts`: 103 passed, 1 failed, exit 1; the one failure
    is G1 and it names that file. A listed file removed (`src/lib/tokens.ts`):
    P1 fails and names it, and that file's break proof fails with a clear
    message (102 passed, 2 failed). Control: the same deliberate line in a
    Stage 2 screen (`Moment1InputForm.tsx`) leaves the test green (104
    passed), which is the rule the header states. The scratch copies were made
    with a root override, `TRIPWIRE_ROOT`, that the test reads; a wrong root
    fails closed.
  - *Two things found by those proofs, both fixed before the commit.* My first
    scratch run was invalid (an unquoted glob in my cleanup command stopped
    the chain, so the folders never existed; I discarded it and redid it). And
    check K6 tested the missing-file check with the real list, so a genuinely
    missing file also tripped it; it now uses its own two-name list. *Limit,
    stated in the file:* the scan looks for the name; it cannot see a row
    spread or a name built from pieces.

### Stage 2 — the form, the settle blanking, the lines, the card

Not started. To record: files changed; RED then GREEN for each new check,
including the blanking, refusal, recognition and shared-person checks, and the
Zone 9 reading check (items 8, 12, 18, 19); the extended row-line tests
(`test:preflight-fold`, `test:tweaks-d`); the full `test:*` result against the
known reds, `test:child-assignment` included; typecheck, format check, build;
the forward check (the
Stage 2 set against the migrated copy); a real browser walk (nothing sends);
known limits (the `AfterThePress` screen); the commit hash; and for the one
deploy, the build log's live-sends line, the migration applied, and the
founder's walk afterwards.

**In progress, 4 Nov 2026.** Only S2.1 is built. The paragraph above is the
plan for the whole stage and is left as filed; what follows is the evidence
for S2.1. It is findings, not rulings.

- **S2.1, the write path of "Decide later": built and committed** (built 3
  Nov 2026, committed 4 Nov; the founder approved the commit in the chat,
  which is an approval and not a ruling). Commit `e923e25`, `feat(GTC-449):
  S2.1 the decide-later marker on all three save doors`, on
  `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only, not pushed, with the two
  attribution lines. Six files, 991 insertions, 18 deletions; this ticket is
  not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran and changed nothing: the
  six committed files have the same blob ids as the six staged ones, and the
  committed diff reads the same as the staged diff saved before (copies were
  saved first). Nothing reads the marker yet (S2.3 will), and a host sees no
  new word.
  - *The rule.* A kid row (a `helpers` entry) may carry `decideLater`. The
    server stores `PersonEvent.messagingUndecided` as "yes" only when the
    stored role is CHILD (the tick is off) and the value is exactly the
    boolean `true`. The tick wins (a ticked row is stored GUEST, marker
    "no"); a value that is left out stores "no" (ruling 20, the safe
    direction); the text "true" does not count; a partner, a guest and the
    host are always "no". There is no server guard after the press (ruling
    25). One small pure function, `decideLaterMarker`, holds the rule for all
    three doors.
  - *The doors and the files.* Households POST
    (`src/app/api/events/[id]/households/route.ts`): `createMember` gained a
    last parameter and writes the marker in its update of an existing person
    and in its create; there are still exactly four `await createMember(tx, `
    calls (the S7 pin of `test:household-save`). Households PUT and
    host-household POST both reach `reconcileHouseholdMembers`
    (`src/lib/households/reconcileMembers.ts`), which now works out the
    marker once per row and writes it in both of its writers. The two route
    files changed in their types only (`decideLater?: boolean`), and
    `src/lib/households/hostHousehold.ts`, which calls
    `reconcileHouseholdMembers`, needed no edit. `LoadedMember` gained an
    optional `messagingUndecided`, typed only, for S2.3. The host row and the
    main contact return before any marker write. `package.json`: one line,
    `"test:decide-later-write": "tsx tests/decide-later-write-test.ts"`,
    last in the `test:*` list (168 scripts); the line before it gains its
    comma. The new file is `tests/decide-later-write-test.ts`: 879 lines, 48
    checks.

- **The throwaway copy** (rulings 94 to 97). `gather_s21_throwaway_gtc449`,
  a local database on the same PostgreSQL server, restored from a fresh
  read-only dump of `gather_dev`:
  `~/gather-db-backups/gather_dev-20261103-2048-s21.dump`
  (183,588 bytes, 336 entries; the dump stays). Its planned name was stated
  in the report before it was made. At the start its 39 tables had the same

  unfinished, and 21 invite events and 0 outbound messages. **It still

- **Red, then green** (3 Nov 2026), in the safe setup, on the copy. The new
  test, `tests/decide-later-write-test.ts`, was written first. It covers
  items 4, 9, 10, 15 and 16 of "The tests". It calls the three real route
  handlers in the test process, with a real user, session and event role,
  and replaces only the cookie reader (`next/headers`), because the
  households POST keeps its member writer inside the route. That is not the
  house habit (the other household tests drive the reconcile function, or a
  dev server over HTTP); it is the way to reach the POST door's writer
  without a server, and it works on a scratch clone for the break proofs.
  - *First red:* 35 passed, 13 failed. Two of my own checks (9.6 and 16.6)
    passed without proving anything, because nothing ever stored "yes". They
    were made to stand alone by saving a "yes" straight into the database
    first.
  - *The red that counts:* 33 passed, 15 failed, exit 1. The 15: 4.2, 4.4,
    4.9, 4.12, 4.13, 4.15, 4.19 and 4.22 (through the three doors the marker

- **S2.3, server half: recognising a settle, and the refusal; built and

  someone who opted out`, on `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only, not
  pushed, with the two attribution lines. The subject is one line of 82
  characters, as the founder gave it. Three files, 1,077 insertions, 3
  deletions; this ticket is not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran
  `prettier --write` over the three files and changed nothing: the three
  committed files have the same blob ids as the three staged ones (copies

  - *The rule.* A settle is recognised in `updateExistingMember` only when
    four things hold: the saved marker is "yes"; the payload carries the
    boolean `false` for `decideLater` (a value left out, `null`, `0` or the
    text "false" does not count); the tick is off, so the role is CHILD; and
    the email arrives blank (missing or empty text). Only then does it ask
    one question: does this person have an `EmailOptOut` row on any event
    (`emailOptOut.findFirst({ where: { personId } })`, through the
    transaction client it was given, by the person alone)? If yes, `email` is
    left out of the person update, so it stays on the record. The phone is
    written as sent (the form blanks it, ruling 17), because a text-message
    STOP belongs to the number (ruling 50). If no, the write is today's,
    exactly: the email is cleared. Every other case is today's write too: a
    stale tab that leaves the marker out, a payload that says `true`, a
    plain child, a ticked row, an ordinary clear, and a row with no
    `personEventId` (ruling 26). The role stays CHILD and the marker is
    stored "no", as S2.1 already did. Nothing is written to the opt-out
    table, nothing is imported from Zone 9, and no Zone 7 file is touched.

- **Finding: the marker read** (accepted as built by the founder; a finding
  and not a ruling). The settle reads the saved marker from the member as
  the edit route loaded it. The `PUT` route loads the household with its
  members (whole `PersonEvent` rows, so `messagingUndecided` comes with them)
  once, just before the transaction opens, and passes them down. The route
  file is untouched by S2.3, and nothing re-reads the marker inside the
  transaction. So a second save that lands between the load and the write
  could leave the value stale. A stale "yes" that is now "no" would be read
  as a settle, which only keeps an email. A stale "no" that is now "yes"
  gives today's write, which clears the email as it does today. Both are
  harmless.

- **The reachability check, in full** (read only; asked of the committed
  code; ruling 100 follows it). The question: after a settle that keeps the
  email, what do the stored reachability fields read, and could anything
  treat that CHILD row as reachable or messageable?
  - *Where the fields come from.* `updateExistingMember` writes the person
    first, leaving `email` out of the update when the email is kept, and
    that update hands back the person as the database now holds them. It
    then calls `reachabilityFor(person.phoneNumber, person.email)` on the
    returned person. It never reads `member.email`, the blank one in the
    payload. So the fields come from the kept email on the person. A settled
    kid with a kept email and no phone is stored contact method EMAIL, tier
    DIRECT. With no kept email it is NONE and UNTRACKABLE, as before.
    `reachabilityFor` reads no role.
  - *The role gate holds on every path.* The chooser
    (`src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`): `chooseAskRoute` and
    `chooseChaseRoute` both ask `isMessageableRole` first; for a CHILD they
    go to the household's carrier, an adult, and never read the child's own
    email or phone; a CHILD with no item is not a recipient. The pre-flight
    route marks every member `messageable` by `isMessageableRole`, so a
    CHILD is false whatever contact details the record holds, and builds
    its chooser candidates from messageable roles only. The ask preview
    (`readAskPreview`) sends every membership through `chooseAskRoute` and
    flags a CHILD with `child: !isMessageableRole`. The press (`pressSend`)
    and the two-minute sender (`drainOnce`) take their recipients from that
    preview and the chooser, not from the stored tier. Resend refuses a
    CHILD with 409 `CHILD_NOT_MESSAGED` before any row is read. The kids
    note (`kidsNote`) lists CHILD rows by name only and reads no address.
    The nudge, wrap-up, manual-nudge and decide-by sweeps are covered by
    `test:child-exclusion` (16/0), whose subject is a CHILD with a valid
    phone, an email and tier DIRECT, with a GUEST control.
  - *The probe, 13 of 13* (measured in the filing session). A scratch script
    kept outside the repo, in memory only, with no database and no network,
    run in the safe setup, put a CHILD row holding a kept email and an
    email opt-out through the real chooser, the real role gate and the real
    kids note: 13 passed, 0 failed. The role gate: `isMessageableRole` is
    false for CHILD and true for GUEST, `isChildMembership` true for CHILD.
    The ask for the kept-email kid is carried to the adult carrier and never
    direct to the kid; a kept-email kid with no item is not a recipient; the
    same holds with no opt-out. The chase never goes to the kid. The control,
    the same person ticked (GUEST): with the opt-out the ask is refused
    (host's list, email opted out), and without it goes direct by email, so
    the gate keys on role alone. The kids note names the kid and carries no
    address.
  - *The finding: two readers count the stored tier and ignore role.* They
    are the reachability breakdown and the trackable-assignment count in
    `src/app/api/events/[id]/invite-status/route.ts` (shown on the plan page
    by `InviteStatusSection` and `ReachabilityBar`), and the compliance
    sweep in `src/lib/workflow.ts` ("Only N% of guests have confirmed", with
    the pending names listed). A search of `src` for `reachabilityTier` and
    `contactMethod` found no other reader. So a settled kid with a kept
    email now counts as "direct" in the bar and as a trackable assignee in
    the compliance rate and its warning, where a kid with nothing on the
    record counts as untrackable.
  - *Said plainly: nothing sends, and the class is not new.* These are
    counts and a warning, and the sweep says in its own comment that it
    blocks nothing. The same happens today to any kid whose email or phone a
    host typed in with the tick off, because `reachabilityFor` reads no role;
    that is the very subject `test:child-exclusion` uses. S2.3 adds one more
    way into it. On the copy, read only in the filing session, all 40 CHILD
    rows read UNTRACKABLE and NONE today. The tier means "has contact
    details", not "may be messaged now": an adult whose email is opted out
    also reads DIRECT.
  - *Ruling 100.* The founder ruled (a), quoted above: leave as built, a
    known limit, no code change. The later-ticket candidate is under "Open
    items", Later items.

**Update, 6 Nov 2026: S2.2, the form, built and committed.** What follows
is the evidence for S2.2 only, filed after the S2.3 server-half entries above.
It is findings, not rulings, except where a ruling is quoted. The lines above
that say S2.2 is not started were true when filed; they are left as filed, and
this entry is the current state. The two notes "staged and not committed" (top
of the ticket) and "Nothing is committed" (Evidence) are still left as filed.

How these facts were sourced. Every fact below was measured in the session
that built S2.2 (6 Nov 2026), from the saved run folders in
`~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/runs` and from commands run in that session,
unless a line says "not re-measured". Two readings of swap, and the first red
run's refusal, were taken before that session's context was summarised and
are marked as taken from its own notes.

- **S2.2, the form: built and committed** (6 Nov 2026; the founder approved
  the commit in the chat, which is an approval and not a ruling). Commit
  `9a6e40f`, `feat(GTC-449): S2.2 the form; a Decide later choice on a kid
  row`, on `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only, not pushed, with the two
  attribution lines. Six files, 1,089 insertions, 5 deletions; this ticket is
  not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran `prettier --write` over the
  six files and changed nothing: copies of the six were saved from the index
  first, and `cmp` finds each committed file byte-identical to its copy. The
  working tree was clean afterwards. All measured.
  - *What the form does.* On a "Kid with a job" row, under the existing tick
    ("Old enough to message directly"), there is a second box, "Decide later"
    (W1). It is offered only while the tick is off. Choosing it, with the kid
    named, shows W2 under it. If the row also holds an email or a phone, W3
    shows too. Choosing it stores nothing and empties nothing: it only
    changes the row, and is sent when she presses Save. Ticking the tick
    turns it off, and unticking does not bring it back. Every kid row is sent
    with `decideLater` as the boolean `true` or `false`, never left out, on
    all three save doors (Save and add another, her own household's first
    save, Save changes). A saved kid on Decide later reopens with the box
    chosen. The browser's kept draft carries the marker, refuses a marker
    that is not a boolean, still restores drafts written before this, and a
    draft that differs from the form only by the marker is put back. Leaving
    the page with only the marker changed asks "Leave site?". The tick wins
    everywhere: a row that somehow holds both reads as ticked.
  - *The files* (numbers from `git show --numstat`).
    `src/components/plan/Moment1InputForm.tsx` (87 insertions, 3 deletions:
    the `decideLater` field on the request and the row, the words as one
    constant, one helper `onDecideLater` that applies the tick-wins rule, the
    snapshot, the draft state and its restore, the saved-household load,
    `buildPayload`, the tick's `onChange`, and the new box with W2 and W3);
    `src/lib/households/form-draft.ts` (4, 1: a draft row may carry the
    marker, and one that is not a boolean is refused, as a bad tick is);

    `tests/decide-later-form-test.ts` (991 lines). No API route, schema or

    `household-channel-choices.ts` needed no edit (founder's confirmed

- **Step 1: the real household list carries the marker** (the founder's
  check, asked after the build, before the commit; no repo file changed).
  - *From the source.* The setup page fetches `GET /api/events/{id}/households`
    and maps `data.households` through `apiHouseholdToSaved` (page line 776).
    That route (`src/app/api/events/[id]/households/route.ts`, GET) reads
    `household.findMany` with `members: { include: { person: { select: ... }
    } }`. There is no `select` on the member itself, so every column of the
    member row comes back, `messagingUndecided` among them. In the schema
    (`prisma/schema.prisma`, `PersonEvent`, line 507) it is `messagingUndecided
    Boolean @default(false)`, so the JSON carries a real boolean. The mapper
    is also fed by four other calls on the page: the host-household POST
    (line 1184), the households POST (1235 and the crowd answer at 1275) and
    the household PUT (1308). Each of those routes also returns its household
    with `members: { include: { person: { select: ... } } }`.
  - *A check that already proves the list.* `decide-later-write` check 4.13
    ("reload: the households GET hands the marker back "yes" for that kid")
    calls the real GET handler and reads the member's `messagingUndecided`
    out of the JSON with `=== true`; 4.4 does the same for the host door's
    GET. Nothing proved the four save answers, and nothing fed a real answer
    to the real mapper.
  - *The form test's sample against the real output.* The test's hand-made
    sample does not match key for key, and should not be read as if it did.
    It is a subset. A real member has 27 keys; the sample has 6 (`id`, `role`,
    `householdRole`, `isYoungPerson`, `person` and, when given,
    `messagingUndecided`), and every one of the 6 is a real key. The 21 it
    lacks (for example `householdId`, `eventId`, `reachabilityTier`, `sentAt`,
    `teamId`) are never read by the mapper. A real household has 8 keys; the
    sample has 5, all real (`createdAt`, `eventId` and `updatedAt` are the
    others). The person has `id`, `name`, `email` and `phoneNumber`, as the
    sample does, and the phone is stored as `+64...` as the sample has it.
    Every key the mapper reads is in the sample under the same name.
  - *The probe.* `~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/probe-s22-real-route.ts`, kept
    outside the repo, run once through the wrapper on the copy. It makes a
    host, two events and four households through the real route handlers,
    reads each answer, and feeds each through the real
    `apiHouseholdToSaved` (read from the page's live source). Two bugs in the
    probe were fixed on the way: the households POST answers 409 until her own
    household is saved first (the probe now does that first), and the list
    holds her own household first (the probe now finds the household by its
    kid). Result: **14 passed, 0 failed.** The households POST, the list GET,
    the PUT and the host-household POST each carry `messagingUndecided` on the
    CHILD as the boolean `true` (and `false` on a plain kid); the real mapper
    turns it into `decideLater: true` for the kid on Decide later and `false`
    for the plain kid and for the ticked kid. After a PUT, the answer still
    says `true`, so an edit save does not clear it from the page's list. The
    last check is that every row the probe made is gone: 0 people, 0 events,
    0 users left. The copy's 39 counts were identical before and after.
  - *Result: the real answers carry the marker.* The step passed, and the
    commit went ahead.

**Update, 8 Nov 2026: S2.3, the form half, built and committed.** What follows
is the evidence for the form half of S2.3 only, filed after the S2.2 entries
above. It is findings, not rulings, except where a ruling is quoted. The lines
above that say the form half is not started were true when filed; they are
left as filed, and this entry is the current state. The two notes "staged and
not committed" (top of the ticket) and "Nothing is committed" (Evidence) are
still left as filed, for the final docs commit.

How these facts were sourced. Every fact below was measured in the session
that built the form half (8 Nov 2026), from the saved run folders in
`~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/runs` (180 runner folders for this step: `s23f-*`,
`s23g-*` and `bp23f-*`) and from commands run in that session. The break-proof
tool is `~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/breakproof23f.cjs`, with the version before
ruling 108 kept beside it as `breakproof23f-v1.cjs`.

- **S2.3, the form half: built and committed** (8 Nov 2026; the founder
  approved the commit in the chat, which is an approval and not a ruling).
  Commit `731b8f5`, `feat(GTC-449): S2.3 form half; settling empties the held
  details, and W9` (72 characters, as the founder gave it), on
  `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only, not pushed, with the two
  attribution lines. Five files, 1,256 insertions, 24 deletions; this ticket
  is not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran `prettier --write` over
  the five files and changed nothing: copies of the five were saved from the
  index first, `cmp` finds each committed file byte-identical to its copy, the
  committed blob ids equal the staged ones, and the working tree was clean
  afterwards. lint-staged's own backup stash was dropped; the two stashes
  still listed are from 5 May and 28 Oct 2026, on other branches. All
  measured.
  - *What the form does now.* On a kid row that was SAVED as Decide later,
    matched by `personEventId` against the saved household the form opened
    with (ruling 26), the email and phone boxes are emptied at once in two
    cases: when Decide later is turned off, and (ruling 108) when the message
    box is ticked and then unticked. Ticking itself empties nothing, because
    the details are the adult's now. Nothing is stored until Save, and Cancel,
    Discard and reopening bring the stored details back. A bad-address or
    bad-number error under an emptied box goes with it. Save then sends the
    settle the server reads: the same kid, `decideLater` false, the tick off,
    and no email and no phone. Nothing is emptied on any other row: a kid
    saved with the choice off or before this change, a decide-later row with
    no `personEventId`, a new row typed with the saved kid's name, or a new
    household's row.
  - *W9 on the card.* After a good save of an edit, the setup page compares
    what it sent with what came back. A kid saved as decide later, sent with
    Decide later off, the tick off and a blank email, whose email comes back,
    is told to the host as W9 on that household's saved card, once for each
    such kid. The line is a quiet one, with no button and no link, and it goes
    when she next opens that household or leaves the page. Nothing is added to
    the server's answer.
  - *The files* (numbers from `git show --numstat`).
    `src/components/plan/Moment1InputForm.tsx` (57 insertions, 3 deletions:
    `savedAsDecideLater`, and the choice's and the tick's change handlers,
    each emptying the two boxes and clearing their errors when they settle a
    row saved as decide later); `src/app/plan/[eventId]/setup/page.tsx` (53,
    1: the module-level pure rule `keptEmailNames`, its use in
    `handleEditSave`, and one effect on `editingHousehold` that drops the
    note); `src/components/plan/HouseholdCardList.tsx` (25, 0: the
    `keptEmailNames` field on `SavedHousehold`, `KEPT_EMAIL_WORDS`, and the
    quiet line; ruling 107); `tests/decide-later-form-test.ts` (980, 13);
    `tests/decide-later-write-test.ts` (141, 7; ruling 106). No API route,
    schema, migration or `package.json` changed, and no new test file or
    script was needed. Nothing under `src/app/api` was edited, so
    `demo-tweaks` B5 had nothing to be red about.
  - *Rulings relied on.* 17, 19, 20, 25 and 26 (the emptying, and which rows),
    33 to 35 (the refusal and W9), 76 (the form half goes with
    `test:decide-later-form`), 93, 94 to 98 and 101 to 105, and now 106 to 109
    (quoted above). The words are W9 exactly as ruled (ruling 35). No other
    new sentence was written.

- **The readings, and which rulings settled them.** The first form-half report

  card, which the plan's file list for this step does not name, and ruling 104

  A saved decide-later kid, ticked and then unticked, ended as "don't message"
  with the details not emptied: asked, and settled by ruling 108. Before
  building it the session said, in the founder's requested plain words: "On a
  kid row that was saved as Decide later, the email and phone boxes are
  emptied at the moment the host unticks the message box after ticking it.
  That is the same moment and the same effect as turning Decide later off.
  Ticking itself empties nothing, and nothing is stored until Save." (c) Built

- **The new checks and what each protects.** The form test went from 50 to 90
  checks (40 new) and the write test from 48 to 52 (4 new).
  - W.2: W9 as the code says it (with {name}) is in the ticket exactly as
    ruling 35 gave it, whitespace folded (see the finding on its limit).
  - 11.12 and 11.13: turning Decide later off on a kid saved as decide later
    empties the email and phone boxes at once; the name stays, the choice is
    off, W2 and W3 go. The same on her own household's edit.
  - 11.14 to 11.17: nothing is emptied on a row not saved as decide later
    (choice off, or saved before this change), a decide-later row with no
    `personEventId`, a new household's row, or a new row typed with the saved
    kid's name. The saved one, matched by `personEventId`, is emptied.
  - 11.18: with two saved kids only the one saved as decide later is emptied,
    and only by its own choice.
  - 11.19: settled to "message" by the tick keeps both details.
  - 11.20 and 11.23: nothing is stored by emptying the boxes: no save, no
    fetch, and the saved household the form was given is unchanged; Cancel
    tells the page, and opening the household again shows the stored details
    with the choice on.
  - 11.21 and 11.22: Save sends the explicit false with no email and no phone
    (the settle the server reads), and a decide-later kid saved unchanged
    keeps both.
  - 11.24 and 11.25: a kept draft holds the emptied boxes, Discard brings the
    stored details and the choice back, and a household put back from a draft
    still knows the kid was saved as decide later.
  - 11.26: an error shown under a box goes when the box is emptied.
  - 11.27 to 11.35 (ruling 108): a saved decide-later kid ticked and then
    unticked is emptied at the moment of unticking (11.27), on her own
    household too (11.28), never on any other row (11.29), only the right kid
    of two (11.30), with nothing stored (11.31), the same settle sent on Save
    (11.32), Cancel and Discard restoring the stored details (11.33), a stale
    error cleared (11.34), and a kid ticked and left ticked sends both details
    as stored (11.35).
  - 18.10 to 18.24: the page tells the host about a kept email only when a
    saved decide-later kid was sent with Decide later off, the tick off and a
    blank email, and the answer still holds one. Not after a settle that
    cleared the email, an ordinary clear, a typed email, a ticked kid, a row
    with no `personEventId`, or a request that leaves the marker out or keeps
    it true. Two kids give a note each, only the saved household changes, and
    the save still closes the form and jumps to the top (18.10 to 18.19,
    18.24). The real card shows W9 exactly as ruled, once for each kid, with
    no button or link, and not otherwise (18.20 to 18.22); opening a household
    drops its note and only its note (18.23).
  - 106.1 to 106.4 (ruling 106): each of the four save answers hands the saved
    marker back as a real boolean on every member row, and a Decide later kid
    reads true (the crowd answer reads false on its four kids). C.1 now also
    counts the crowd's made-up people.

- **Findings** (the founder's instruction; findings, not rulings).
  - *The `handleEdit` pin.* `demo-tweaks-3` T4 holds `handleEdit` equal to the
    page at the tag `demo-tweaks-2-good`, comments stripped and whitespace
    flattened. The S2.0 census and build rules had missed it. So W9 is cleared
    by a small effect on `editingHousehold` when a household is reopened, and
    not in `handleEdit`. It is added to the S2.0 build rules as a missed pin.
  - *Ruling 106, as read.* "Both households POSTs" was read as the plain
    households POST and the crowd POST ("Add a crowd", the route's second
    shape): the two places in the setup page that map a households POST answer
    (`handleAddHousehold` and `addCrowd`). The crowd answer cannot hold a
    Decide later kid, because it is made-up people with no payload, so check
    106.2 pins the marker as the boolean false on all 4 kids and on every
    person in its 11 households. It needs a sample copy: an `isDemo` event, a
    throwaway account at the reserved `demo-copy.invalid` domain, and her own
    household saved first. The test makes that made-up sample event on the
    copy and removes it, and the crowd's people, by id; C.1 counts them.
    Mutation Q10 (the test forgets the crowd's people) turned C.1 red, left 24
    strays on the copy, and the harness removed exactly those by id; the
    copy's counts then matched again. (Its log line says "25 removed": that is
    psql's own "DELETE 24" tag line counted as a row.)
  - *W9 once for each refused kid, and a stale error cleared with its box.*
    Two kids refused give two lines, each naming its own kid (checks 18.18 and
    18.22). A bad-address or bad-number error under a box that is emptied is
    cleared with it, by turning the choice off (11.26) or by unticking
    (11.34). Both were built without a question and are pinned.
  - *Turning Decide later back on does not restore emptied details.* After the
    boxes were emptied, choosing the choice again leaves them empty. Cancel,
    Discard or reopening the household brings the stored details back. Not
    pinned.
  - *The W9 whitespace limit.* No check reads the code's sentence
    (`KEPT_EMAIL_WORDS`) against the ticket directly. The chain is the ticket,
    then the test's own copy of the sentence (W.2), then the card as drawn
    (18.20 and 18.22), and each link is proved: a changed ticket turned W.2
    red (W2), a reworded card line turned 18.20 and 18.22 red (C1), and a
    curly apostrophe did too (C2). But W.2 folds all whitespace and the card
    checks fold it too, so a stray double space in the sentence would pass.
    The founder ruled against a standing exact check. A one-off comparison on
    8 Nov 2026 (not a check) found both places in the ticket (ruling 35's
    quote and the W-lines entry) identical to the code's sentence once the
    ticket's line break is read as a space: 82 characters, none of them
    outside ASCII.
  - *The corner in ruling 108.* Unticking empties whatever is in the boxes at
    that moment, including text typed while the box was ticked, which is what
    "the same as when Decide later is turned off" gives. Not pinned either
    way.
  - *Check 18.10 is red on arrival and has no break proof of its own.* It only
    tests that the page has a `keptEmailNames` function. Its absence also
    turns 18.11, 18.18 and 18.19 red (R0 and mutation P8), and nothing else
    the page does depends on its name.

**Update, 8 Nov 2026: S2.4, the confirm line, built and committed.** What
follows is the evidence for S2.4 only, filed after the S2.3 form-half entry
above. It is findings, not rulings, except where a ruling is quoted. That
entry's last section says S2.4 is "the next step" and "not started"; that was

How these facts were sourced. Every fact below was measured in the session
that built S2.4 (8 Nov 2026), from the saved run folders in
`~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/runs` (154 runner folders for this step: 78 `s24-*`
and 76 `bp24-*`) and from commands run in that session. The break-proof tool
is `~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/breakproof24.cjs`, a copy of `breakproof23f.cjs`
with its own mutation list and without the write test.

- **S2.4, the confirm line: built and committed** (8 Nov 2026; the founder
  approved the commit in the chat, which is an approval and not a ruling).
  Commit `87f9c3a`, `feat(GTC-449): S2.4 the confirm line; asks before a kid
  is messaged` (67 characters), on `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only,
  not pushed, with the two attribution lines. Three files, 547 insertions, 9
  deletions; this ticket is not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran
  `prettier --write` over the three files and changed nothing: copies of the
  three were saved from the index first, `cmp` finds each committed file
  byte-identical to its copy, the working files match too, and the tree was
  clean afterwards. All measured.
  - *What it does, in plain words.* After the invitations have gone out
    (`Event.sentAt` is set, ruling 91), "Save changes" on a saved household
    does not save at once when a kid who was saved as Decide later is now
    ticked "Old enough to message directly". In the place of the button the
    form draws ruling 89's line, with the kid's name in it, and two buttons:
    "Save and let Gather message {name}" and "Go back". Nothing is stored
    until the first is pressed, and it then saves exactly as "Save changes"
    does, with the same checks. "Go back" closes the line and saves nothing,
    keeps everything typed and does not reset the form. Cancel is unchanged.
    It is a screen courtesy only: the request is the same with or without it,
    and no server file changed (ruling 25).
  - *When the line appears.* All three must hold: the event has been pressed
    (`Event.sentAt` set); the host presses "Save changes" on a saved household
    (her own household too); and at least one kid row was saved as Decide
    later (matched to the saved household by the row's `personEventId`, the
    S2.3 test, ruling 26) and is now ticked.
  - *When it must not appear, and does not.* Before the invitations have gone
    out (the setting false, or not passed at all); a plain kid ticked after
    the press (ruling 16); a kid saved before this ticket, with no marker; a
    kid left on Decide later; a kid settled to "don't message" (the choice
    turned off, or ticked and then unticked); a new row typed in this sitting;
    a kid matched by name only (two kids called Josh); "Save & add another
    household"; her own household's first-screen save; and a ticked kid with
    no name. "Save & move on" is not drawn while a household is being edited,
    so it cannot reach this.
  - *The names (ruling 111).* `joinNames`, a small exported function in the
    form file: one name as it is, "Josh and Mere", "Josh, Mere and Aroha" (no
    comma before "and"), each name trimmed. The names are the kids saved as
    Decide later and now ticked, in the order of the form's list of kid rows;
    both the line and the Save button carry them. A ticked kid with no name is
    left out (see the findings).
  - *The approved defaults.* If anything in the form changes while the line
    shows, the line closes and "Save changes" comes back, and putting a box
    back as it was does not bring the line back. The form remembers the form
    as it stood when she was asked, and a change makes it differ. No sentence
    was added beyond ruling 89's line and two buttons.
  - *The files* (numbers from `git show --numstat`).
    `src/components/plan/Moment1InputForm.tsx` (95 insertions, 8 deletions:
    the optional `invitationsSent` prop, `CONFIRM_WORDS`, `joinNames`, the
    list of names, the remembered form and the effect that closes the line,
    the new `pressSaveChanges` and `confirmSave`, and the line and its two
    buttons drawn in place of "Save changes");
    `src/app/plan/[eventId]/setup/page.tsx` (1, 0:
    `invitationsSent={Boolean(event.sentAt)}` on the call that edits a
    household, ruling 110); `tests/decide-later-form-test.ts` (451, 1). No API
    route, schema, migration, `package.json` or other source file changed, and
    no new test file or script was needed. `handleEdit`, `handleEditSave`,
    `resumeEdit` and `apiHouseholdToSaved` are untouched. Nothing under
    `src/app/api` was edited, so `demo-tweaks` B5 had nothing to be red about.
    (The `--stat` column shows lines added plus lines removed, 95 + 8 = 103
    and 451 + 1 = 452; the S2.4 report's file list wrongly gave those as
    insertions. See "Said plainly".)
  - *Rulings relied on.* 5, 16 and 25 (the line, for decide-later kids only, a
    screen courtesy), 89 (the words), 91 (the signal), 110 and 111 (quoted
    above), 26 (rows matched by `personEventId`), 76, 93, 94 to 98 and 109.
    The words are ruling 89's, exactly. No other new sentence was written.

- **The readings, and which rulings settled them.**
  - The plan's S2.4 entry names one file, the form. The form cannot know that
    the invitations have gone out, cannot fetch (a `household-save` pin), and
    the S2.0 build rules say the flag goes in as a new prop and not through
    `resumeEdit`. So the page had to pass it, which the entry did not name.
    The report asked and the founder ruled (ruling 110).
  - The line is asked before the form's own checks run. The pins
    (`household-save` S1 and S2) count the text `if (checksFail()) return;` at
    four and `hasValidationErrors()` at one, and S9 holds the bodies of
    `handleAddAnother` and `handleEditSave` to the commit `9ff914d`, so the
    question sits in a new click handler and the checks run when the line's
    Save button is pressed. With a bad address the host sees the line first,
    presses Save, and then sees the address error; check 5.13 pins that.
  - "In the order they appear in the form" was read as the order of the form's
    list of kid rows. A new row goes to the top of the screen, but a new row
    can never be named, and saved rows keep their saved order, so for the kids
    who can be named this is the order on screen. Check 5.11 pins it by
    ticking in reverse. A restored browser draft was not separately checked.
  - The line's Save button carries ruling 89's label, "Save and let Gather
    message {name}", while the line shows, and reads "Saving…" while the save
    runs, as "Save changes" does. That is not a new sentence.

- **The throwaway copy** (rulings 94 to 98 and 109). Every run was on
  `gather_s21_throwaway_gtc449`, reused, not remade and not dropped. Its 39
  table counts were taken at the start and at the end and equal `gather_dev`'s
  at both times (counts only, ruling 109); neither list changed between the
  two.

- **Red, then green** (8 Nov 2026), in the safe setup, on the copy. The form

    new row last, but the form puts a new row first; and P.2 asked two things

    only call that passes it", P.2 only "the first-screen call does not"). Two

- **The new checks and what each protects** (33, in `test:decide-later-form`;
  items 5 and 14 of "The tests", with the words, the joining and the page).
  - *W.3* (green on arrival). Ruling 89's line and its two buttons, as the
    code says them (with `{name}`), are in the ticket exactly. A word changed

  - *J.1 to J.6.* The name joining: the function exists and is exported; one

  - *5.1 and 5.2.* After the press, a kid saved as Decide later and now ticked

  - *5.3.* "Save and let Gather message Josh" saves once, to the same

  - *5.7 to 5.9.* Anything changed while the line shows closes it; putting a

  - *5.10 to 5.12.* Two kids read "Josh and Mere"; three, ticked in reverse

- **Green on arrival, with break proofs.** Of the 33 new checks, 21 were red
  on arrival and 12 were green on arrival (W.3, 14.1 to 14.10 and P.2). Every
  one of the 12 was proved by a mutation written for it, and every one of the
  21 was also killed by a mutation aimed at its property.
  - *How.* A full local clone of the repo in
    `~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/work/clone-s24`, outside the repo, with the
    working tree's changes copied over. One change at a time, the form test
    run on the copy in the safe setup after each, then the clone put back. A
    control first and last (123 passed, 0 failed both times), and run R0,
    which puts the form and the page back to the commit before this step. The
    repo's status and diff are hashed before and after: identical in both
    passes (`c6dc5f897268` in the first, `78c45953cfc1` in the final one).
    Each pass was 38 runs: the control, R0 and 35 mutations, and the control
    again; every run read sentinel 21 and 0, migrations 59 and 0, and an
    unchanged database list.
  - *The 35 mutations.* Ruling 89's words changed in the ticket only (the
    line, the Save button: 2) and in the code (the line, the Save button, "Go
    back": 3). `joinNames` (6): not exported; a comma before "and"; not
    trimmed; two names with a comma; "&" for "and"; one name with a trailing
    "and". Who is named (10): the press ignored; any ticked kid; matched by
    name; ticked not needed; a nameless kid counted; the add-household door
    asking and not saving; her own household's save not going through; a kid
    with the choice off counted as going to message; the names reversed; only
    the first name. What the line does (10): it asks and also saves; "Go back"
    resets the form; does nothing; saves; the line's Save button never saves;
    an extra field in the request; the line returning when a box is put back;
    the line staying whatever changes; the line not closed before the save
    runs; a nameless kid hiding the line. The page (4): always true; another
    field of the event; not passed; passed on the first-screen call too.
  - *Two passes.* In the first, every target was killed except 14.4: the
    mutation that ignores the press made an older section of the test (18)
    crash before 14.4 could run. That is a flaw in how the proof was arranged,
    not in the check. The new block was moved ahead of section 18, so its
    checks are logged before any such crash, and the final pass proved all 12.
    The "setting not passed" half of 14.4 is also caught by 11.35 in the same
    mutation, where the older section fails.

- **Findings** (findings, not rulings).
  - *A ticked kid with no name (ruling 111; accepted as built).* The existing
    checks: every Save runs the form's checks first, and a kid row with no
    name gets "Name is required" under it, which stops the save. That is so
    for any kid row, ticked or not. So a nameless kid can never reach a save,
    and the line has no name to put in it. Built: such a kid is left out of
    the names and no sentence is added. Alone, no line shows and "Name is
    required" stops the save (14.1); beside a named ticked kid, the line names
    only the named one, and pressing its Save button then meets "Name is
    required" (5.14). Mutations C5 and D10 prove both checks can fail.
  - *The line is asked before the checks.* See the readings. It follows from
    the pins and is pinned by 5.13; a later change would need the pins to
    move.
  - *What the line does not do.* It does not make a link or send anything. Its
    words promise only that Gather "will be able to" message the child, as
    ruling 89 chose; what a save does after the press (the link is made when
    the host next opens "Invites & people") is F9 above and is unchanged.
  - *The look of the line has not been seen in a browser.* It reuses the
    classes of the "Save changes" and "Cancel" buttons and sits in the page
    flow (no fixed overlay). The browser suites were not run (they belong to
    S2.9). The founder's walk of the form at S2.9 should include a pressed
    event and a decide-later kid ticked.

    yes | local: yes", sentinel 21 and 0 for invite events and outbound

    loaded with 0 refusals in the runs that print it.
  - Every run: Node 22 through the guard stand-in, provider keys blank,
    `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS` set nowhere, the OS-level wall (`sandbox-exec`,

    string printed, and no connection to `gather_demo`. The only reads of
    `gather_dev` were the two count passes (ruling 109).
  - Two dev servers in turn, inside the wall, on port 3000, each for the four
    server-needing old checks. The guard's shared refusal log shows 14

    12 times and `registry.npmjs.org` twice (Next.js's own telemetry and update
    check, as in S2.1, S2.3 and S2.2), none of which reached anywhere. Both

- **S2.6, the card marker: built and committed** (8 Nov 2026; the founder
  approved the commit in the chat, which is an approval and not a ruling).
  Commit `a98377c`, `feat(GTC-449): S2.6 the card marker; shows a kid on
  decide later` (64 characters), on `feat/contact-import-screen`, local only,
  not pushed, with the two attribution lines. Three files, 471 insertions, 3
  deletions; this ticket is not in it. The hook (lint-staged, Prettier) ran
  `prettier --write` over the three files and changed nothing: copies of the
  three were saved from the index first, `cmp` finds each committed file
  byte-identical to its copy, the working files match too, and the tree was
  clean afterwards. All measured. At the founder's request the staged numbers
  were quoted before the commit: 2, 20 and 449 added, and 1, 2 and 0 removed.
  - *What it does, in plain words.* On a saved household card, a kid who was
    saved as Decide later reads "Josh (decide later)": the name, one space,
    then W8's words (ruling 14). It is display only: nothing is saved, sent,
    linked or counted. No server file, API route, form or setup-page file
    changed, because the flag already reached the card from S2.2 (the page's
    `apiHouseholdToSaved` sets it, and ruling 104 gave the card its type line).
  - *Where it shows.* On the kid line of a saved card, once for each kid saved
    as Decide later, straight after the name. It shows on her own household's
    card too (the host look and the "You" pill are unchanged). A flagged kid
    with no name shows "(decide later)" alone after the kid icon (ruling 113).
  - *Where it does not, and does not change anything.* A kid who is not
    flagged draws exactly the old markup (`<span>👦 Josh</span>`), whether the
    flag is false, undefined or absent. A partner, a guest and the primary
    contact never show it, even given a stray flag. The dashed "not saved
    yet" card and the "Editing" card, which show what is typed, never show it.
    Little kids counted by number have no names and no flag, so they never
    show it. The words live in one exported constant, `DECIDE_LATER_WORDS`,
    beside `KEPT_EMAIL_WORDS`; the span carries `data-decide-later-note` as a
    hook for the tests.
  - *The look (ruling 114).* Plain text in the line's own size and grey, with
    no new colour or class. It has not been seen in a browser.
  - *The files* (numbers from `git show --numstat`).
    `src/components/plan/HouseholdCardList.tsx` (20 insertions, 2 deletions:
    the constant, the kid line, and one doc comment above `decideLater`
    reworded because it still said "S2.6 shows it");
    `tests/decide-later-preflight-test.ts` (449, 0; new, ruling 112);
    `package.json` (2, 1: the line `test:decide-later-preflight` after
    `test:decide-later-form`, and a comma on that line). No API route, schema,
    migration, setup page or form changed. Nothing under `src/app/api` was
    edited, so `demo-tweaks` B5 had nothing to be red about.
  - *Rulings relied on.* 14 (W8 approved, part of the ticket), 104 and 107 (the
    card file's earlier changes), 76 (the test and its script), 112 to 114
    (above), 88 and 92 (one commit and a separate docs commit), 93, 94 to 98
    and 109. The words are W8's, exactly. No other new sentence was written.

- **The new checks and what each protects** (16, in the new
  `test:decide-later-preflight`; the W8 part of item 13 of "The tests").
  - *W.1* (red on arrival). The card's words are "(decide later)", and the
    ticket's W8 holds "Josh" followed by exactly them. A word changed on one
    side only goes red.
  - *W8.1* (red). A kid saved as Decide later shows "Josh (decide later)" on
    the kid line, once, name first, with a space between, in a plain `span`.
  - *W8.2 and W8.3* (red). With two kids and one flagged, only that kid shows
    the words. With two flagged, each shows them once.
  - *W8.4* (red). With two households, only the one holding a flagged kid
    shows the words.

    pill, shows them too.

    live source) and drawn by the real card. The CHILD with the marker "yes"
    shows the words. The CHILD with "no", an adult-roled youth (stored GUEST)
    given a stray "yes", and a plain guest do not.

- **Green on arrival, with break proofs.** Of the 16 checks, 9 were red on
  arrival and 7 were green on arrival (W8.8 to W8.14). Every one of the 7 was
  proved by a sabotage written for it, and every one of the 9 by a sabotage
  aimed at its property.
  - *How.* A full local clone of the repo in
    `~/gather-gate-runbook/s21/work/clone-s26`, outside the repo, with the
    working tree's changes copied over. The harness, `breakproof26.cjs`, is in
    the same runbook folder and is never committed. One change at a time, the
    new test run on the copy in the safe setup after each, then the clone put
    back. A control first and last (16 passed, 0 failed both times), and run
    R0, which puts the card back to the commit before this step. The repo's
    status and diff are hashed before and after: identical in both passes
    (`1e2fc13d3e65` in the first, `9a60ed479fd3` in the final one). Each pass
    was 35 runs: the control, R0 and 32 sabotages, and the control again;
    every run read sentinel 21 and 0, migrations 59 and 0, and an unchanged
    database list.
  - *The 32 sabotages.* The words (3): a hyphen in the code; a hyphen in the
    ticket only; a capital D. Where it shows (12): no space after the name; the
    words before the name; the words written twice; the stored column name
    read in place of the flag; every kid; one flagged kid making the whole
    household show it; only the first flagged kid; once any kid is flagged,
    every later kid, across households; hidden on her own card; hidden when
    nameless; the page's mapper never setting the flag; the mapper ignoring
    the role. Where it must not show and what must not change (9): an
    attribute on every kid's span; a false flag drawing a stray element; the
    people line counting a flagged kid twice; the partner, a guest and the
    primary contact each showing it given a stray flag (3); the guard removed
    so the typed and editing cards show it; only the new card guarded; only
    the editing card guarded. Display only (8): the words as a link, as an
    inert button, as a working button and as a form field; a call to `fetch`
    in the card; the router named in the card; a click handler on the words;
    W9's line hidden when a flagged kid is present.
  - *Two passes.* The first was run before the test file was formatted and
    the final one after, on the text that was committed. Both killed every
    target. Unlike S2.4, no sabotage made an older section crash before a new
    check could run.
  - *Which sabotage killed which green-on-arrival check.* W8.8: every kid
    shown, an attribute on every span, a false flag drawing an element. W8.9:
    the false flag, the people line. W8.10: the three adult rows. W8.11: the
    guard removed, only the new card guarded. W8.12: the guard removed, only
    the editing card guarded. W8.13: link, inert button, working button, form
    field. W8.14: link, working button, fetch, router named, click handler.

    still matches and nobody's ticks vanish at the deploy; a kid newly on decide

- **Two checks red only on an uncommitted tree, before and after the commit**

- **Findings** (findings, not rulings).
  - *Unhoused people.* The marker is returned and fingerprinted for people
    outside any household too, but W5, W6 and W7 are per household and count
    only household members, so an unhoused person on decide later would be on no
    line. The marker is only written through the household forms, which always
    put a kid in a household, so this should not arise; checks R.3 and T.5 pin
    that the field is true to the record there.
  - *A stale tick.* A kid newly saved as Decide later changes step 3's
    fingerprint, so a step 3 tick made earlier drops, and Ready's with it. That
    is the intended behaviour (the entry's "a change elsewhere un-ticks the
    step") and is pinned by T.1. Nobody's existing tick drops at the deploy
    (T.2).
  - *The look of the lines has not been seen in a browser.* W5 and W6 reuse the
    "Kids:" line's classes (ruling 115) and W7 the amber of `talkLine`'s line
    (ruling 28). The founder's walk at S2.9 should include a household with a
    decide-later kid, step 3 folded and an open household, and one more
    household with no one to talk to, to see both row lines.
  - *What the lines do not do.* They make no link and no button, add nothing to
    Send's rule, and say nothing on `AfterThePress`, which is a later item
    (ruling 22).
  - *`tweaks-d`'s K3 and the cut.* See "The browser-mixed suites". It is red
    whenever layer C is cut, at clean HEAD too.

- **The safety numbers.**
  - 369 runner folders for the step: 85 for the runs by hand, the batches and
    the clone suites, and 284 for the two break-proof passes (71 trials of two
    runs each, per pass). Every test run went through `runcopy.cjs`, which stops
    with exit 70 unless the connection points at the copy, and with exit 71 if a
    baseline is wrong before the run; none stopped. Every run line read sentinel
    21 and 0 for invite events and outbound messages, migrations 59 and 0, and
    "database list: unchanged". The batch and break-proof tools stop at once on
    a stop condition, and none fired.
  - Every run: Node 22 through the guard stand-in, provider keys blank,
    `GATHER_LIVE_SENDS` set nowhere, the OS-level wall (`sandbox-exec`,
    loopback only), nothing sent, never production, no key or connection string
    printed (the `.env` files were checked for presence only: `.env` present,
    `.env.local` absent), and no connection to `gather_demo`. The only reads of
    `gather_dev` were the two count passes at the start and the end (ruling
    109). The database list was also read by hand through the maintenance
    database (ruling 98) and was unchanged. No cron or scheduler ran, and no
    browser was driven.
  - Two dev servers in turn, inside the wall, on port 3000: one for the second
    old-check pass and one for `demo-sandbox-b` after the commit. Both were
    stopped; ports 3000 and 3100 had no listener afterwards. The guard's shared
    refusal log holds 49 lines in all, 42 for `telemetry.nextjs.org` and 7 for
    `registry.npmjs.org` (Next.js's own telemetry and update check, as in S2.1
    to S2.6), none of which reached anywhere. It is shared across steps and no
    count was taken at the start of this one, so the part that belongs to this
    step is not known.
  - Swap read 0 MB at every check made by hand and in every run that prints it,
    against the limit of 10,800 MB.

- **Said plainly, as it happened.**
  - The first red run listed 32 failures, not 33: S.10 was red for the wrong
    reason, and S.4 and S.5 wrongly passed (the empty-join guard). All three
    were slips in the test's own text, fixed before the build.
  - In an interim report the first old-check pass was called 33 runs; it was 31
    (29 green, 2 red). The log was counted afterwards and this entry has the
    right number.
  - One of Claude Code's own commands, a `sed` edit of the break-proof harness,
    failed on a shell quirk; the chain stopped, so the harness did not start,
    and the line was fixed with the edit tool before it ran. Two read-only

  - A `BASE=1` mode was added to the clone helper, to run a browser-mixed suite

  - In plan mode, one `git fetch --dry-run` was run. It contacts the remote to

  - The label text of `tweaks-d` P1 and P2 (and `tweaks-f` P1) is now slightly
